### Elias Artista <note>Elias Artista is a legendary figure in alchemical lore, particularly within the Paracelsian tradition. He was prophesied to appear during the "last days" to reveal all the hidden secrets of nature and alchemy.</note>

->With<-

# The Philosopher’s Stone. <note>In alchemy, the 'Philosopher’s Stone'—here called the 'Stein der Weisen'—is the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life or spiritual perfection.</note>

->2 Chronicles 13:5<-

> Do you not know that <term>Jehovah</term> <note>The personal name of God in the Hebrew Bible.</note>, the <term>God of Israel</term> <note>original Hebrew: *Elohim Israel*</note>,
> gave the kingdom over Israel to David,
> to him and to his descendants, with a

## Salt-Covenant. <note>A 'Salt-Covenant' (Salz-Bund) refers to a biblical promise that is eternal and incorruptible, as salt was a symbol of preservation. In alchemical philosophy, Salt is one of the 'Three Primes' (Tria Prima) along with Sulfur and Mercury, representing the body or the principle of fixity.</note>

> forever.

## Elijah the Artist <note>original: "Elias Artista." This refers to a messianic figure in alchemical lore, first prophesied by Paracelsus, who was expected to appear at the end of time to reveal all hidden knowledge and perfect the chemical arts.</note>

# The Philosopher's Stone.

### 2 Chronicles 13:5.

> Do you not know that Jehovah, the <term>Elohim</term> <note>A Hebrew name for God, emphasizing His power as Creator and Judge.</note> of Israel, has given the kingdom over Israel to David, to him and to his seed <note>Refers to his descendants or lineage.</note>, with a

## Covenant of Salt. <note>In the Bible, a "Covenant of Salt" (Salz-Bund) signifies a permanent, incorruptible, and unbreakable bond. In alchemy, salt is one of the three prime substances (Tria Prima) representing the body and fixity.</note>

> eternally.

# Preface.

Experts in chemical writings <note>The author uses "chemical" in its older sense, referring to the "Hermetic" or alchemical arts.</note> will know that Paracelsus, Philalethes, and perhaps others besides, have written of Elias the Artist <note>original: "Elias Artista," a legendary figure in alchemy predicted to appear in the end times to reveal the secrets of the "Great Work."</note>, that he would come. God’s Word says that Elias will come shortly before the Last Day, and will restore and set all things right. Malachi 4:5; Matthew 17:11; Mark 9:11.

But what shall Elias set right and restore? Surely nothing else but the doctrine of wisdom in the whole Word of God in its true sense, as can be seen in the priests of Baal <note>original: "Baals-Pfaffen," a derogatory term used here to describe clergy who follow false or corrupt teachings.</note> in the false doctrine, whom he must bring to an end. All secret and hidden wisdom originates from Jesus Christ, our God and Father Adam <note>This reflects a mystical belief that Christ was the "New Adam" or the divine prototype of the first man.</note>, who wrote the books of the Word of God, as well as the books of other hidden arts and sciences, such as astrology, alchemy, and the like, with his own hand at the beginning of the world, when he walked upon this our earth as a true man, as has been proven elsewhere. He wrote everything, however, in images, riddles, and parables, which during the time of the external church up to now have been led into erroneous and false doctrines; and this is what Elias is to restore and set right, for before the end of this our coarse earth, everything shall yet be revealed: There is nothing hidden, that [will] not [be] revealed

<summary>This preface introduces Elias the Artist, a messianic figure in alchemy destined to restore the true, hidden meaning of divine wisdom and science before the end of the world. The author claims that all secret arts like alchemy were originally written by Christ in the form of Adam using parables.</summary>
<keywords>Elias Artista, Paracelsus, Philalethes, alchemy, astrology, secret wisdom, Adam, Christ, end times, parables</keywords>

# TO THE SPIRIT OF PENITENCE <note>original: "Dem Geist der Buße" - The term "Buße" implies both internal repentance and the outward act of penitence, a major focus of 18th-century spiritual literature.</note>

# Preface.

Those familiar with chemical <note>original: "chymischen" — in the 18th century, this term encompassed both chemistry and alchemy.</note> writings will know that **Paracelsus**, **Philaletha**, and perhaps others besides, have written of **Elias Artista** <note>Elias Artista (Elias the Artist) was a legendary figure in alchemy, first predicted by Paracelsus, who was expected to appear in the "last days" to reveal all secrets of nature and medicine.</note>, saying that he shall come. God’s Word says that Elijah shall come shortly before the Last Day and shall restore and set all things right again. Malachi 4:5; Matthew 17:11; Mark 9:11.

What then shall Elias restore and set right? Certainly nothing other than the doctrine of wisdom in all of God's Word in its true sense, as can be gathered from the Priests of Baal <note>original: "Baals-Pfaffen" — a derogatory term for clergy whom the author considers to be following false or idolatrous teachings.</note> in their false doctrine, to whom he must bring an end. All secret and hidden wisdom originates from Jesus Christ, our God and Father Adam <note>The author here appears to blend the figures of Christ and the primordial Adam, a concept found in esoteric Christian theosophy where Adam is seen as the original possessor of divine knowledge.</note>, who wrote the books of the Word of God, and also the books of other hidden arts and sciences—such as **Astrology**, **Alchemy**, and the like—at the beginning of the world with his own hand, when he walked upon this our earth as a true human being, as has been proven elsewhere. However, he wrote everything in images, riddles, and parables, which during the current era of the external Church have been led into erroneous and false doctrines; and that is what Elias is to restore and set right, for before the end of this our gross earth <note>original: "groben Erde" — referring to the material, unrefined physical world.</note> everything shall yet be made manifest: There is nothing hidden that shall not become manifest <note>A reference to Luke 8:17.</note>

<summary>The author explains the prophecy of Elias Artista, an alchemical messiah who will restore the "true sense" of divine wisdom—including alchemy and astrology—which was originally written by Adam but obscured by false religious teachings.</summary>

<keywords>Elias Artista, Paracelsus, Philaletha, alchemy, astrology, Adam, prophecy, Priests of Baal, hidden wisdom, Last Day</keywords>

# Preface.

Those familiar with chemical writings <note>original: "chymischen Schriften" — In this 18th-century context, this refers specifically to alchemical texts.</note> will know that Paracelsus, Philaletha <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes was a famous 17th-century alchemist whose identity remained a secret.</note>, and perhaps others besides, have written of Elias Artista <note>The "Elijah of the Arts" or "Elijah the Artist," a prophetic figure in alchemy expected to appear before the end of the world to reveal the ultimate secrets of nature.</note>, saying that he is to come. God’s Word says that Elijah shall come shortly before the Last Day <note>original: "Jüngsten Tage" — the Day of Judgment.</note> and shall restore and set all things right again. Malachi 4:5, Matthew 17:11, Mark 9:11.

But what is it that Elijah is to set right and restore? Certainly nothing other than the doctrine of wisdom in all the Word of God in its true sense, as may be discerned from the priests of Baal <note>original: "Baals-Pfaffen" — a derogatory term used here for those teaching "false" religious doctrines.</note> in their false doctrine, to whom he must put an end. All secret and hidden wisdom originates from Jesus Christ, our God and Father Adam <note>The author suggests a mystical connection where Adam, as the first man, held all divine knowledge directly from Christ.</note>, who at the beginning of the world wrote with his own hand the books of the Word of God, as well as the books of other hidden arts and sciences—such as Astrology, Alchemy, and the like—when he walked upon this our earth as a true man, as has been proven elsewhere. He wrote everything, however, in images, riddles, and parables, which during the era of the external Church up to this time have been led into erroneous and false doctrines; and this is what Elijah is to restore and set right, for before the end of this our coarse earth, everything shall yet be made manifest: There is nothing hidden that shall not be revealed. <note>A reference to Matthew 10:26 or Luke 8:17.</note>

<summary>The author introduces the prophetic figure of Elias Artista, who is destined to restore the true, hidden meaning of God’s Word and the ancient sciences (Alchemy and Astrology) originally written by Adam.</summary>
<keywords>Elias Artista, Alchemy, Astrology, Adam, Paracelsus, Philaletha, Last Day, Biblical prophecy, restoration of wisdom</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the introductory matter, moving from the symbolic title page (A 1) to the formal preface (A 2). It establishes the theological and alchemical framework for the book, focusing on the prophecy of a coming restorer of wisdom.</meta>

# Preface.

Connoisseurs of chemical <note>original: "chymischen" — In the 18th century, "chemistry" and "alchemy" were often used interchangeably, though the context here is clearly the spiritual and transformative art of alchemy.</note> writings will know that **Paracelsus** <note>The Swiss physician and alchemist (1493–1541) who revolutionized medicine and predicted the coming of a great reformer.</note>, **Philaletha** <note>likely Eirenaeus Philalethes, a famous 17th-century alchemist whose name means "peaceful lover of truth."</note>, and perhaps others besides, have written of **Elijah the Artist** <note>original: "Elias Artista" — A messianic figure in alchemical tradition expected to appear before the end of the world to reveal the ultimate secrets of nature and alchemy.</note>, saying that he shall come. God’s Word says that Elijah shall come shortly before the **Last Day** and shall restore and set all things right. **Malachi 4:5; Matthew 17:11; Mark 9:11.**

What, however, is Elijah meant to restore and set right? Certainly nothing other than the teaching of **wisdom** in all the Word of God in its true sense, as can be gathered from the **priests of Baal** <note>A biblical reference to false prophets; here, it refers to those who teach "false" or purely external religious doctrines.</note> in the false teaching, whom he must bring to an end. All secret and hidden wisdom originates from **Jesus Christ, our God and Father Adam** <note>The author presents a radical theological view where Adam, the first man, is identified with the divine and credited with writing the foundational texts of all sciences.</note>, who wrote the books of the Word of God, and also the books of other hidden arts and sciences, such as **Astrology, Alchemy**, and the like, with his own hand at the beginning of the world when he walked upon this our earth as a true man, as has been proven elsewhere. He wrote everything, however, in images, riddles, and parables, which during the current era of the **outer church** <note>Refers to the visible, institutional church, which the author believes has lost the deeper, "inner" spiritual meaning of the scriptures.</note> have been led into erroneous and false teachings; and that is what Elijah is to restore and set right, for before the end of this our **coarse earth** <note>original: "groben Erde" — Refers to the physical, material world in its fallen state, which alchemists believed would eventually be refined or spiritualized.</note>, everything shall yet be revealed: There is nothing hidden that [shall] not [be] revealed

# Preface.

...nothing secret that will not be made known and come to light. **Luke 8:17.**

Since all secret wisdom comes from God, the art of the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>original: "Stein der Weisen" — The legendary substance in alchemy capable of transmuting base metals into gold and serving as an elixir of life; here, it also carries a deep spiritual meaning.</term>, which has been so hidden until now, also comes from God, who described this art in images and riddles at the beginning of the world. And because Elijah is to restore and reveal everything in its true sense, he must also present the images and riddles of the **Philosopher’s Stone** in their true sense, and reveal the **Philosopher’s Stone** and its preparation, which is done in this book. **Paracelsus** <note>The Swiss-German physician and alchemist (1493–1541).</note> especially recognized that this should happen before the end; therefore, he named the man through whom it happens **Elias Artista** <note>original: "Elias Artista" — Elijah the Artist, a predicted figure who would reveal the secrets of nature.</note>, which name it shall keep, and for that reason, I call this book, *Elijah the Artist*.

Let no one think, however, that this revelation of the **Stone** happens so that it may benefit people through the making of gold and silver! Oh no, for these idols of the earth will come to a complete end with the now-approaching **Last Day** <note>The Day of Judgment or the end of the world.</note> and the terrible **Judgment**, and will no longer exist in the future; why then would one want to make gold and silver? Thus, no one will save their gold and silver on this **Day of the Lord**, but everyone will cast away their golden and silver idols. **Isaiah 31:7.** Therefore, this revelation of the **Philosopher's Stone** concerns only the revelation of the secret

## Chapter I.

->concerning the<-

## First Matter of all Things.

<note>original: "Prima Materia." In alchemy, this refers to the primitive, formless base of all matter required for the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

->§. 1.<-

<image-desc>A decorative vignette on the left side of the paragraph serving as an ornamental initial "P" for the word "Paracelsus". It depicts a landscape with trees and a central shield bearing the letter "P", surmounted by an ornate, lidded urn decorated with garlands.</image-desc>

Paracelsus wrote in his day that he was the monarch of wonders and secrets, and said: "The monarchy has come upon me; follow me!" In his time, he could say this with truth, for he was one of the greatest sages who ever lived. But now Elijah <note>Referring to "Elias Artista," the same prophetic figure mentioned in the preface.</note> says: "The monarchy has come upon me; follow me!" I shall speak from an entirely different school than all the **Adepts** <term>Adepti: individuals who have successfully achieved the "Great Work" of alchemy and discovered the secret of the Stone.</term> who existed before me have spoken and written.

# On the First Matter of all Things.

<note>original: "Prima Materia." In alchemical philosophy, this is the formless, primordial substance from which all things are created and to which they can be reduced.</note>

->§. 1.<-

I will grant each man his due merit, but I will not allow their chaff to pass for wheat. They commonly say that the *prima materia* of the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>The Philosopher's Stone: A legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and providing the "elixir of life."</term> is the *prima materia* of all things, yet in this, they speak as the blind do of colors. I will first demonstrate the *prima materia* of all things, and thereafter we shall see the *prima* <gloss>typo for materia</gloss> *materia* of the Stone itself.

Since nothing can exist or be made without Nature, and all things must necessarily stand within Nature, I must first explain what Nature is. I do not wish to hide these mysteries but will investigate them from the beginning of Creation. I will not bypass the truth, for God has granted me the ability to speak wisely and to think rightly according to such a gift of wisdom. He has given me certain knowledge of things, such that I know how the world is made and the power of the elements. <note>original: "Weish. 6. u. 7." This refers to the Book of Wisdom (Wisdom of Solomon), chapters 6 and 7, which describe God's gift of wisdom and knowledge of the physical world.</note>

## Nature.

->§. 2.<-

Nature is an ever-living and self-moving essence that consists of four primary qualities or properties, which are named **Fire**, **Air**, **Water**, and **Earth**. However, we must not restrict ourselves solely to the concept of coarse elements, for these are merely the casing and the coarse offspring of the true elements, which are pure spirit and life.

The element of **Earth** is a contracting, hard, thick, and body-forming property of Nature; it is the encloser and container of all other elements. It is the ground and cause of all **salts** <term>Salts: In alchemy, Salt is one of the "Three Primes" (Tria Prima), representing the principle of stability, fixity, and the physical body.</term>, which are therefore also contracting, sharp, and body-forming. Thus, the element of Earth, along with Salt, is the primary foundation and the basis of Nature, in which all its powers [rest] together

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>The author distinguishes between the universal First Matter and that of the Philosopher's Stone, defining Nature as a living essence composed of four spiritual elements, beginning with a detailed description of Earth as the container of all things and the source of Salt.</summary>
<keywords>First Matter, Prima Materia, Nature, Elements, Fire, Air, Water, Earth, Salt, Tria Prima, Wisdom of Solomon</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the four elements and their role in the creation of the Philosopher's Stone. It picks up from the previous page's exploration of the element of Earth, which the author equates with Salt.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>
<header>On the First Matter of all Things.</header>

...which lie gathered together, and therefore Salt is also the main subject of the Stone, as we shall see in the following.

The element of Air is opposed to the element of Earth, just as their chemical symbols <term>chemical symbols: Alchemical signs used to represent substances; Fire is an upright triangle, Water is an inverted triangle, Air is upright with a horizontal bar, and Earth is inverted with a horizontal bar.</term> stand opposite one another. It is the expanding, emerging, penetrating, and refining property of Nature, which halts and prevents the contraction, thickening, and hardening of the element of Earth, so that Nature does not stand still, nor freeze in death, but remains an eternally living and moving being. The two elements of Air and Earth constitute body and life; for just as Salt or the element of Earth is the body of all things; so too is Air the life of all things. Air—or the World Spirit <term>World Spirit: Known in Latin as the *Spiritus Mundi*, this was believed to be a universal life force or subtle substance found in the air that could animate or "give life" to alchemical preparations.</term>—is therefore also the other part of the Philosopher’s Stone, and it consists entirely of Air and Salt, and nothing else is added to it.

The element of Fire is the warming and drying property of Nature and is most closely related to Air. It reveals its properties through movement, because all movement creates warmth and heat, and it also helps to strengthen that movement.

The element of Water is opposed to Fire, just as their chemical symbols stand opposite one another. It is the moistening, softening, and beneficial property of Nature that restrains the property of Fire, so that it does not ignite in fury—in its sharp, biting, consuming, heating, and drying property. It soothes and creates quiet rest, and is thus, along with Earth, opposed to the restless movement of the two higher elements, Fire and Air. Fire thus belongs to Air; therefore, Air is the "fire" within the Philosopher’s Stone, and no other fire may be added to it, as we shall see hereafter.

->§. 3.<-

These four elements, or four primary properties of Nature, exist together in an inseparable bond; they struggle with

<page-num>10</page-num>
### On the First Matter of all Things.

...one another, seek and desire one another, move and drive one another, and taken together, they constitute a single living and eternally moving Nature, which is the life of the world. None of these properties can exist without the others, otherwise there would be an end to Nature; just as there is an end to all temporal natures when one of these four properties exalts itself too much and suppresses the others, such that they must break, at which point external life comes to an end. This is thus the description of Nature, and it is correct.

There is no living and moving being without these four properties or four elements. If something is to be, and is to have a certain essence and being, then it must consist of certain properties which constitute its essence and being; and that is Nature in the aforementioned four elements. Nature thus consists of properties of compatibility <note>German: *Annehmlichkeit*. In this context, it refers to the way the elements 'receive' or 'accept' one another to form a harmonious whole.</note>, where one affects and touches the other, from which the intelligent spirit is then born, which is conscious of a thing and perceives, through the five senses or in a fivefold way, what is within its nature and essence.

Without Nature, nothing can be created, and from nothing, nothing comes <note>original: *ex nihilo nihil fit* - a classical philosophical principle.</note>. This is a firm truth, and whoever wishes to deny it, let him show me a single thing that was made without Nature and out of nothing, and then I shall praise him. The expressions in Scripture about "bringing something out of nothing" are misunderstood. They only mean to say that a specific being is brought forth from a universal being, and a subsequent thing is produced from the preceding one. Sixty years ago, I did not yet exist either, but that from which I became was indeed there: namely, the nature of the world and my parents. Therefore, I did not come from nothing, and no thing in the world has ever come from nothing; rather, one has proceeded from the other. But Scripture says of God: "From him and through him are all things" <note>original: Romans 11:36. The author uses this verse to argue that because God is the source—and God is "Everything"—creation cannot truly be said to come from "Nothing."</note>. But God is no "nothing"; therefore, no thing has ever been created out of nothing.

<header>On Nature, and the First Matter <term>First Matter: Latin *prima materia*; the primal, formless substance from which all things in the universe were created.</term> of All Things.</header>

->§. 4.<-

Since all things have come forth from God, and yet nothing can exist or be created without Nature, then God Himself must also be a Nature. And because no Nature can exist without four elements, the four elements must also be in God. The four living creatures <note>original: *Thiere*. These are the four "beasts" described in Revelation 4:6-8.</note> in the Apocalypse and the four wheels in Ezekiel <note>referring to the "wheels within wheels" in Ezekiel 1:15-21</note> before the throne, or within the throne of God, are nothing other in the eternal being of God than the four eternal elements in the eternal Nature. If someone does not wish to believe this, let him tell me then, what are they? And if someone will not admit that the being of God also exists within a Nature with four elements, let him tell me then what God is, and whether He is not a living and self-moving being? Can such a being exist without these four principal properties of Nature? And does not God’s Word itself attribute a nature to our God when it says that we shall become "partakers of the divine nature"? <note>A reference to 2 Peter 1:4.</note> God is therefore Nature, and for that reason there is a temporal and an eternal Nature; indeed, there are various temporal principal natures of the world, all of which are meant simultaneously under the name of God, and yet one is not the other. Is not even the Devil himself called by the name of God—namely, the god of this world? <margin>2 Cor. 4, 4.</margin> Are we not all also subject to the general Nature of the world as to a god, and do not all external things have their being and life from the external Nature of the world? So that I may show the harmony of the Philosopher’s Stone <term>Philosopher's Stone: The legendary substance of alchemy, here used to represent the perfected "seed" or essence of the universe.</term> with the whole world, I will first briefly describe the Creation here.

Imagine the eternal Nature of God before the creation of this world like our air, which is spread out over the entire globe,

### The Mystery of God with the Creation of the World.

...and in which our lower nature is also contained. This eternal nature, which is thus spread through the entire world, is the eternal being of God.

The purpose of Creation was that this eternal divine being, with its eternal powers, wished to introduce itself into as many fixed human creatures as possible. For this purpose, He formed Himself into a human image and shape, and the nature of God spread throughout this entire world worked toward this, just as even now the entire nature of the world must work together to form every human being. This first creature, or this "firstborn of all creation," <note>original: *Erstgebohrne von aller Kreatur*. A reference to Colossians 1:15, identifying the primordial man as the blueprint for all humanity.</note> was Jesus Christ, our father Adam, and that is the personal God. <note>original citations: Colossians 1:15, 1 John 5:20. The author is blending the figures of the biblical Adam and the divine Christ into a single "God-Man" archetype.</note> The being of God spread throughout the entire world nevertheless remained what it was and where it was, but the personal God rules over this universal being; for this Father had given over all power and authority to the Son.

This was the creation within eternal nature, in which, however, no more than this one creature—this God-man—could be created. For us humans, however, there was no other possibility that we could be created in multitude, except within a temporal nature, and indeed in a nature that had to stand in the utmost imperfection, just as we have been begotten in such a nature until now. To this end, God undertook three main outer creations in this world, which are as follows.

->§. 6.<-

The first outer creation was the creation of the fixed stars <term>Fixed stars: In pre-modern astronomy, these were the stars that appeared not to move relative to one another, as opposed to the "wandering stars" or planets.</term> and the pure angels. This was the expansion of God’s powers with stars and angels, in a number as great as the humans He wished to create.

The second outer creation was the creation of the planets, among which our earth also was; for it is one of those

...six celestial bodies orbiting the sun, but at that time it was still in its most pure condition.

The third outer creation was the six-day creation of our Earth into an even more external state, and this creation concerned only the lower world, which extends to the circle of the moon and no further. On the first day, nature was created as it still is today, above our firmament of clouds up to the circle of the moon. On the second day, an even more external nature was created, namely the nature above our Earth up to the firmament of clouds, where the clouds and the rain begin to be born. On the third day, a bodily essence was added to the already existing Earth. And here, in this creative act, the nature of the first day (which also surrounded the body of the Earth) turned completely into a nature of fire; and that is the satanic nature, which was indeed immediately enclosed within the interior of the Earth, but was later released during a further change of the Earth into corruption <note>A reference to the Fall of Man and the subsequent "corruption" of the physical world.</note>, whereupon it has since extended to our firmament of clouds, but no further. *Ephesians 2:2.* <note>The author cites this verse to identify Satan as the "prince of the power of the air."</note> This nature was absolutely necessary for the creation of man, because it had to awaken the opposite <note>original: *Contrarium*</note> of nature on Earth, without which the procreation of human beings was not possible.

->§. 7.<-

This is the very brief description of Creation, of which one finds everything proven and described in detail in the *Book of the World* <note>A common mystical concept suggesting that the physical universe is a "book" written by God that can be read by the enlightened.</note>; for our purposes here, this may suffice to show the harmony of the Stone of the Wise <term>Philosopher's Stone: in this context, a spiritual catalyst or symbol of divine perfection</term> with the creation of the world. The outer world had to have three main creations before the multiplication of the human race could occur; and these three main creations, in each of which a whole nature of the world was created (though only within its own sphere): these, I say, were first the foundation for the further creation of man. And

<page-num>14</page-num>

# The Mystery of God with the Creation of the World.

...just so, the preparation of the **Stone of the Wise** <term>The Philosopher's Stone: In alchemy, this is the legendary "tincture" or substance capable of perfecting both metals and the human soul</term> also has three **rotations** <note>Alchemical cycles of heating, cooling, or distillation</note> in the preliminary work, in which the foundation for the Stone is first laid; and then, in the subsequent work, follows the **multiplication** <note>The process of increasing the quantity and power of the alchemical medicine</note> or the increase of its potency.

The preparation of the Stone of the Wise is, however, a **new creation**, a rebirth from the lower and corruptible into the upper and spiritual, whereas the previously described creation of the world proceeded from the upper into the lower. Therefore, because the Stone is a new birth, it is also the most perfect image in nature of **Jesus Christ**, the spiritual cornerstone.

->§. 8.<-

Into all these creations, Jesus Christ—our father **Adam**—personally entered, and in each one, he took such a nature upon himself. Thus, man possesses all the primary natures of the world within himself, which have been propagated from Adam to his children and so from one human being to another. These natures lie within man one inside the other, where one is the covering over the other, and the coarse physical body is the outermost covering over all the others. As it is in man, so it is also with the natures in the world, as the natures or essences of God; there, too, one lies within the other. Thus, the eternal nature of God is also present in the nature on Earth and in every human being, but hidden deep within the interior, as if under various coverings or different natures. The holy essence of God does not enter into corruption; however, when the renewal of nature on Earth now comes, and the satanic nature is bound and cast out, then the holy power of God will penetrate all beings and natures on Earth. This, then, is the **rebirth of the world**, which steps back from all things temporal into the eternal through the three great primary stages, just as it stepped down; and of this, the preparation of the Stone of the Wise is the true and perfect reflection.

<header>The Mystery of God with the Creation of the World.</header>

### §. 9.

One nature of the world has proceeded out of another through the act of creation, and all of them together are called by the name **God**, just as all the various natures in a human being are called by the name **Man**. However, just as the coarse physical body is not the true essence of humanity, so too the external **Nature-God** <note>The author distinguishes between the visible, physical universe (the "body" of God) and the hidden, eternal spiritual essence of the Creator.</note> is not the holy, eternal God. Yet we live and move within God, for with our external selves we live in the external Nature-God; we were formed from it and cannot live or exist without it. With our inner selves, however, we live in the eternal God; we were formed from Him and can have no spiritual or divine life within us without Him.

Because everything is made out of nature, and nature is the beginning of all things—yet there are various kinds of natures and beginnings—there is also more than one kind of **prime matter** <term>original: *prima Materia*; the fundamental, raw starting substance from which a specific thing is formed</term> for all things. We can seek the prime matter of the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>, and we shall see that it is by no means the same as the prime matter from which man was created, as some **adepts** <term>original: *Adepte*; individuals who claim to have mastered the secrets of alchemy</term> have babbled aimlessly without understanding what they were saying themselves.

The prime matter of the Stone is of no higher nature or essence than the outer air and coarse salt; for it is made from these and nothing else, as we shall see in what follows. Prime matter is also described by the adepts as a **chaos** <term>A state of primordial, unorganized matter</term>, in which everything supposedly lay together in a confused jumble, from which God then created things in an orderly fashion. But this confused chaos existed only in the brains of those people and nowhere else. All things proceeded from God, and God was no confused chaos. Each higher nature was the prime matter or "chaos" for the lower nature that followed it, and each was already created in an orderly state before the next one came, and was no confused

<summary>The author explains that while the physical world is an expression of God, it is not His eternal essence, and argues that the "prime matter" of the Philosopher's Stone is derived from simple elements like air and salt rather than a mythical primordial chaos.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s critique of traditional alchemical definitions. Having established that creation is a process of one nature emerging from another, the author now refutes the idea of "chaos" as a void or a "nothing," specifically targeting the definitions provided by the famous physician and occultist Paracelsus.</meta>

<page-num>16</page-num>

<header>The Mystery of God with the Creation of the World.</header>

...coarse chaos. In the riddles of wisdom, there are indeed such images of a chaos, but truly no **adept** <term>original: *Adept*; a person who claims to have mastered the secret "Great Work" of alchemy</term> has understood their true meaning. When a preceding nature has been set to create another, its properties have had to enter into conflict and turmoil, through which a subsequent nature was formed. As soon as this new nature was achieved, however, the properties of nature returned to a state of balance and rest, both in the first and the second; for turmoil and unrest exist only during the act of creation itself. Because creation has been ongoing until now, our nature on Earth has always been a certain kind of chaos, in which the higher forces flowing in from above lay mixed with the properties of the lower nature, which were then formed into physical beings. In this way, there was a chaos from which things were created—and in no other way. In the act of creation itself, nature immediately organizes itself into order, just as we see in the growth of plants and the formation of a child in the mother's womb.

### §. 10.

Paracelsus calls the <term>prime matter</term> <note>original: *prima Materia*; the fundamental, raw starting substance of all reality</note> of all things the "great mystery" and says it was not comprehensible and had no property, color, or elementary nature; it supposedly existed in no essence or image, and the elements were only created from it afterward. One might well call that a chaotic description of chaos! If it was without essence, property, color, image, and elementary nature, then it was a <term>non-entity</term> <note>original: *Non ens*; a philosophical term for a thing that has no existence</note>—a nothingness. The elements must surely be present before anything can be created, for the elements constitute nature, and without nature there is nothing, and from nothing, nothing is made. This description of his regarding chaos is a nothingness; therefore, he described it correctly, for there was no such chaos to begin with—and so his description matches his "nothing" of a chaos perfectly. Paracelsus was one of the great—

...greatest wise men, and I do not cite this to belittle him, but only to show how in past times even the best people mixed much chaff into their teachings, and that one must not rely on them in all things. If this is the case with such great men, what then will be the case with other **adepts** <term>original: *Adepten*; those who have mastered the "Great Work" of alchemy</term> who were but children compared to **Paracelsus** <note>original: *Paracelsum*; the famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and occultist</note>? Our air is, in a certain sense, a chaos in which the forces of all things—all herbs and fruits—lie mixed together without one being able to say: this or that is grain, wood, metal, or herb. However, one must not, for that reason, place such a confused chaos at the beginning of the world and claim the world itself and all things were made from it.

The **prime matter** <term>original: *prima Materia*; the fundamental starting substance of the alchemical process</term> of the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>original: *Steins der Weisen*; the legendary substance capable of transmuting metals and healing the body</term> is, as has been said, nothing other than salt and air; it extends no further than that. For this reason, the Philosopher’s Stone serves only the external life of the air and the health of the body; indeed, it cannot even preserve that once the term of a person's life has arrived, for all adepts have nonetheless had to die.

### The Second Chapter.

## Proof that the Philosopher’s Stone is made from Air and Salt, and specifically from Tartar and Saltpeter.

->§. 1.<-

Because everything in nature exists within the **four elements** <note>Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</note> and is made from them—yet nature must be living and active if it is to create or even improve anything—so too must the matter of the Philosopher’s Stone be of such a nature that nature [is] living...

and be active within it. This [life] cannot be present in ores or minerals and metals, for these have died off from the life of nature, no longer have any connection to it, and therefore can no longer grow. In salt, however, living nature can exist and has access to it, for the **spirit of the world** <term>original: *Geist der Welt*; often referred to in alchemy as the *Spiritus Mundi*, a universal life force or "breath" believed to permeate all things</term> can be attracted through salt; indeed, one can attract so much of it that it **coagulates** <term>original: *coagulirt*; the process of a liquid thickening or becoming solid</term> into a fiery water, filling entire bowls with such water.

Everything produced on earth is produced through the number 2 and exists in duality: as life and body, from the upper and the lower, from a **subtle and a coarse** <note>This refers to the alchemical distinction between refined, spiritual substances and heavy, material ones</note>, from an **active and a passive** <note>original: *Wirkenden und Leidenden*; the Aristotelian principle that change requires an agent to act and a subject to be acted upon</note>, from male and female, and from heaven and earth. Who can deny this? If, then, the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>original: *Stein der Weisen*; the ultimate goal of alchemy, representing perfection and the union of opposites</term> is to be the most perfect image of the entire universe, it must also have two parts that appear clearly distinct to the eye, yet can nonetheless be united with one another, like man and woman, or like heaven and earth.

->§. 2.<-

The number 2, which is most evident to every rational creature, is spirit and body, or life and body. Now, however, the air is the life of all things, and the salt is the body of all things. If one burns all physical things, a salt will remain in the ashes, which is the foundation of physicality—indeed, the enduring foundation that lasts even longer than coarse, dark matter. For this reason, salt appears white, and the color white is the image of heaven; therefore, salt is to be regarded as a "heavenly earth" in contrast to coarse black matter.

In salt, the element of earth has the upper hand, as has already been proven, because it creates and preserves physicality. For this reason, it is also the encloser and container of all living nature, for the body indeed encloses the life within itself. Because now the

<page-num>19</page-num>

<header>Salt is a primary part of the Philosopher’s Stone.</header>

Salt is the body of all things, as one sees that all physical things have a salt within them; thus salt is universal or common to all things. And because the air is also the life of all things—of both **plants** <note>original: *Vegetabilien*</note> and **animals** <note>original: *Animalien*</note>—it is also common or universal to all things. Therefore, the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting eternal life</term> is called a universal stone and a **universal medicine** <term>a "panacea" believed to cure all ailments by restoring the body's internal balance</term>. For that reason, it can be nothing other than salt and air, because one finds no other two things that are both common to every single thing in the world.

With these two pieces, we therefore possess everything: the whole of nature within each of these two—the upper and the lower, heaven and earth, life and a salty body. From this, it must necessarily be possible through the **Art** <note>Refers to *Ars Hermetica*, or alchemy, viewed as a sacred craft that assists nature in reaching perfection</note> to create the noblest and best thing on earth—indeed, a new heaven and a new earth. For when heaven is united with earth, and God with the creature, nothing but pure blessing and goodness can arise. A person may contemplate and think as they wish, but they will find nothing in all of nature that is more essential and necessary than air and salt; for without air there could be no life, and without salt no body could exist or persist. However, because there are many various types of salts, and one might still be in doubt as to what kind of salt must be taken, it shall now be proven that it must be salt of tartar and saltpeter.

## Salt of Tartar or Cream of Tartar.

->§. 3.<-

The fundamental **Hermetic rule** <note>A reference to the *Emerald Tablet*, a foundational text of alchemy attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note> for the Philosopher’s Stone states: what is **Above** is like that which is **Below**, and what is **Below** is like that which is **Above**, and from the union of both, the miracles of a single thing happen, or occur within a single thing.

<page-num>20</page-num>

# Tartar and Saltpeter are the Subject of the Stone.

Above is heaven and life, and Below is the earth and the body, both of which we have previously demonstrated to be found in air and salt. This fundamental rule teaches us, firstly, that one must possess two things which must be united into one. Anyone who wishes to make the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note> would therefore first need to reflect on whether they indeed have two things, what those things are, and how they may be joined into one. Secondly, this fundamental rule teaches us to look at the "great world" <note>The macrocosm or universe</note> with its "Above" and "Below," and to take two things which nature itself creates and provides. These, as mentioned, we have in air and salt. However, because nature provides many different types of salts, and the **Philosopher’s Stone** requires the most perfect and essential substances for its matter, we must look for the most perfect among all salts—the one which possesses a more perfect origin than others. This is **Salt of Tartar** <term>Potassium carbonate, specifically that derived from the crusty deposits in wine barrels; it was highly valued in alchemy for its purity</term>.

Wine is the most perfect "water" that the nature of the world itself produces, and from wine comes **tartar** <note>original: *Tartar*. A bypass-product of winemaking</note> salt. Grape juice comes directly from the fiery **spirit of the air** <note>The "pneuma" or life-force believed to be carried in the atmosphere and captured by the vine</note>, and therefore so does the salt of tartar. Consequently, none of all the salts is more closely related to the "Above" than this one, with the exception of **saltpeter** <term>Potassium nitrate; alchemists believed it contained a "nitrous" celestial salt from the air</term>, which will be discussed hereafter. Thus, one can say of no other substance with greater truth than of this: **what is Below is like that which is Above** <note>A reference to the famous Hermetic maxim from the *Emerald Tablet*: "Quod est inferius est sicut quod est superius"</note>. It is also the most perfect salt because wine itself serves as nourishment and strengthening for human nature. Man is the most perfect creature, and that which serves for his strengthening and preservation must necessarily also be more perfect than other things that do not unite with human nature; therefore, for this reason, no salt is as perfect as tartar.

->§. 4.<-

The order of creation testifies to us that the physical arose from the spiritual, and the coarse from the subtle.

# Tartar and Saltpeter are the Subject of the Stone.

Dry earth is created through separation from water, or by the setting aside of the moist, watery kind, and we still see today that the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Weltgeist*; the universal animating force or "pneuma" in alchemical theory</note> thickens within rainwater; but when this is left to stand, an earth settles within it, in which the salt resides. Likewise, the Spirit of the World thickens in the juice of the grape, and from this fiery water of nature, **tartar** <note>original: *Tartarus*; the potassium bitartrate crystals that form in wine casks</note> is separated. Just as wine is more perfect than rainwater, and just as the salt of tartar is produced in a more perfect way than any other salt, it is also that much more perfect than all other salts <note>original: *Salien*; a Latinate plural for salts</note>.

Because wine is the most precious juice of the Spirit of the World, and the Spirit of the World is universal to all things, so must salt of tartar also be the most precious salt; it must be universal to all things in a closer degree than other salts, and must therefore be the most suitable for the **Universal Medicine** <term>A legendary substance, often synonymous with the Philosopher's Stone, believed to cure all diseases and prolong life</term>, because wine is so closely related to human nature and strengthens it. But those are fools who, because of this close relationship between wine and human nature, wish to make the **Philosopher’s Stone** from the dung and urine of a person who drinks much wine. Human nature absorbs the good power of the wine, while it expels the refuse through urine and other natural excretions. How then do such **filth-philosophers** <note>original: *Schmutz-Philosophen*; a derogatory term for alchemists who worked with base organic matter or "vulgar" materials</note> intend to make the **Philosopher's Stone**—which is supposed to contain the best of heaven and the best of earth—out of the stench and filth that nature itself vomits out?

Salt of tartar is an "immediate earth" from the most fiery atmospheric water, a magnificent origin which all other salts lack. It must therefore also be of a drier nature than all others. It is a dryness that comes from a fiery water of nature. It is a dry fire that comes from water. ## Saltpeter.

->§. 5.<-

Now we have both the "Above" and the "Below" with complete certainty, and can say of both with the foundation of truth: *that which is Above is like that which is Below, and that which is Below is like that which is Above.* <note>A translation of the famous Latin maxim from the *Tabula Smaragdina* (Emerald Tablet): "Quod est superius est sicut quod est inferius."</note> But now we must also look to the following words, namely: *from the union of both, the wonders of a single thing occur.*

This union requires an equality of natures, for "like seeks like," and one of such natures finds joy in the other; just as a youth and a maiden are most eager to unite with one another.

We already have this equality of natures in the **Air** and the **Salt of Tartar**. However, because the Tartar is dry, fixed, and closest to the element of **Earth**, having separated from its mother—that is, from Air and Water—and indeed being separated from the Air in the third degree, it no longer has a connection with the Air as the "Life of the World." It is contrary in quality: for as the "Below," it is dry and fixed, whereas the "Above," like the Air, is moist and volatile.

The union of both must naturally be accomplished through the **Art** <note>original: *die Kunst*; the specific laboratory practice of alchemy</note>, for if no Art were required, then all things would become the **Philosopher’s Stone** on their own. Because

# Tartar and Saltpeter are the Subject of the Stone

now that these two parts no longer have any connection with each other in the life
of the world; it follows that the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, often called the "Hermetic Art."</note> must add something to the Tartar which
lies in death, so that this dead youth might once again achieve a
desire for his bride, and so that this dead Adam might again obtain
a longing for Christ as for life. But that which is to be added
to the Tartar must be contrary neither to it nor to the Air
in its natural kind. Therefore, one must seek a middle salt <note>original: *Mittelsalz*; an intermediate or neutralizing salt that acts as a bridge between two substances.</note>,
which is just as closely related to the Air as the Tartar is
to the Earth. When this is united with the Tartar,
it makes it capable of longing for the Air again, attracting
it, and being able to unite with it. This, however, is **Saltpeter**,
for it is an "air-salt" <note>original: *Luftsalz*; alchemists believed saltpeter was condensed from the atmosphere.</note>, and often attaches itself of its own accord from
the air onto walls or other places of the earth where urine or
the like has fallen. Compared to other salts, Saltpeter is to be
regarded as a volatile salt, and it unites so willingly with the
Air that it very easily becomes damp and wet from it. For this
reason, the materialists <note>original: *Materialisten*; here referring to merchants or druggists who dealt in raw chemical materials and minerals.</note> who trade in saltpeter do not like to
place it in damp places, so that it does not attract [moisture] and become wet.

->§. 6.<-

Saltpeter taken alone would not be enough for the bodily
part of the Stone, because it has too little of the **fixity** <term>Fixity (Fixität): The quality of a substance that allows it to remain stable and non-volatile when exposed to high heat.</term> of the element Earth,
and corresponds more to the Moist than to the Dry in its nature.
The Tartar, however, taken alone, would also not be
suitable for it, because it lies too much in fierce death and hell,
and is separated from the life of the world. In the divine books
of Wisdom, hell is also called **Tartarus** <note>The author is making a linguistic and symbolic connection between "Tartar" (the chemical) and "Tartarus" (the Greek mythological abyss or hell), a common wordplay in Paracelsian alchemy.</note>. And
just as the satanic nature reversed itself in its formation
and separated itself from the spiritual light and life; even so has
the **wine-stone** <note>original: *Weinstein*; another name for Tartar.</note> entirely separated itself from the wine, with which
Christ compares himself, and has become a fierce essence to
human nature, even though wine is its [nature's] strengthening.

# Tartar and Saltpeter are the Subject of the Stone

But just as the satanic nature, or the hellish Tartarus <note>Alchemists often punned on "Tartar" (the wine crust) and "Tartarus" (the Greek underworld or hell), viewing the crude mineral as a trapped, "hellish" state of matter that needed redemption.</note>, has until now had to serve for the creation and creaturely production of the children of men, and has also held the upper hand on earth; even so must the Salt of Tartar also serve for the Philosopher's Stone, and must help to form the bodily and creaturely part of it in the preliminary work. Indeed, there must be more of the Tartar than of the Saltpeter.

When Tartar and Saltpeter are melted together over a charcoal fire, the Tartar is made eager for the Air by the Saltpeter, and its "mouth" is, as it were, thawed open, so that it can hunger again for its origin from whence it came, and attract the Air. When both have flowed together and are poured out, it becomes a white salt, hard as a stone. It has, however, become so eager for the Air that even if left on a table in a room, it becomes soft in a short time, and gradually attracts and coagulates the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Weltgeist*; believed to be a universal vital force or "mercury" present in the atmosphere.</note> into a quantity of clear water. Such a water is useful neither for health nor for the Stone, but is rather harmful to both; yet one can already see the power of <term>Tinting</term> <note>original: *Tingiren*; the alchemical ability to change the color and quality of a substance, often a sign of successful transmutation.</note> from it, in that a few drops of such water placed in white wine will color the wine blood-red.

->§ 7.<-

Now we have that which is required for the Philosopher’s Stone. We have the best of Heaven, which is the **Life**, and we have the best of Earth, which is the **Body**. From the joining of both, the best thing in the world must necessarily be able to be made, if only the matters are arranged correctly for it.

These are, therefore, the three <term>Principles</term> <note>original: *Principia*; the three fundamental components of matter in the Paracelsian tradition: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note> of the Philosopher’s Stone: **Salt**, **Sulfur**, and **Mercury**—that is, Saltpeter, Tartar, and the Spirit of the World—and there are absolutely no others. This I testify.

To be sure, I have no need for the eyes of others, nor can the eyes of others help me if I do not see for myself. Before that, however, I wish to remind the reader that throughout this entire writing, whenever I use the word **Tartar** in relation to the Stone, I always mean <term>crystals of tartar</term> <note>Known today as potassium bitartrate or cream of tartar, this substance was seen by alchemists as a "fixed" salt of the earth found in wine barrels.</note>.

The highly enlightened Paulus Felgenhauer <note>A 17th-century German mystic and visionary (1593–c.1677) who wrote extensively on the intersection of theology and nature.</note>—who, as he says, was not a practicing Adept, but was nonetheless a man of exceptional knowledge of nature—spoke the following regarding **Salt**:

> "The spoken and embodied Word," (namely, the flowed-forth and created Nature,) "through which the world was created, is a Salt; for Salt is the <term>concentrated center</term> <note>original: *Centrum concentratum*</note> of all elements, a coagulated and congealed **Heavens** <note>original: *Schamajim* (Hebrew for "heavens"). Alchemists often interpreted this word as a combination of *esch* (fire) and *majim* (water), representing a "fiery water."</note>, and is a physical Word of creation. When Salt, in its proper vessel and container, is breathed upon by a pure air, it becomes water; for it is a coagulated water, and water is a dissolved Salt. One may well say that Salt is the best and the good in creation—indeed, it is all in all. Nothing can endure in the fire except Salt, from which hereafter a new heaven and a new earth shall come. <term>Sulfur</term> <note>In alchemy, sulfur represents the soul and the principle of combustibility.</note> is a physical fire; Salt makes it endure in the fire. In Salt there lies much hidden mystery, and whoever does not know what lies in Salt, in which the power of all things resides, will know little of nature."

Likewise, concerning **Saltpeter**:

> "The Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil is, in its essence, a wondrous Saltpeter of the Object; where something breaks or divides, it is hellish and heavenly, evil and good."

### Saltpeter, Tartar, and Air are the Principles of the Stone.

Here one should remember that **Tartar** <note>original: *Tartarus*. In alchemical thought, the "hellish" sediment of wine barrels, representing the lowest or most fixed material.</note> is compared to the hellish essence, and the **Air** to the heavenly. Because **Saltpeter** is the middle ground between both and participates in both, it is rightly compared to the **Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil**. He further says:

> "The cold watery fire is the **Spirit of Saltpeter**, a spiritual water from the salt which simultaneously contains heat and cold within itself. Saltpeter is a wondrous salt that has no equal, and a great secret is hidden within it, more than one might believe. But the fierce, terrible hell that is in Saltpeter must be flooded, drowned, swallowed up, and transformed into a blessed Heaven in its own consuming and devouring fire. When, therefore, a heavenly water is present, then a new birth can occur from above out of **Water and Spirit**. A great medicine lies hidden here, etc. And indeed, the time is near when everything must be revealed. Although we ourselves have not yet prepared it with our own hands, the Spirit of Wisdom can and may teach us all."

->§. 2.<-

There has never been a man in the world who wrote more or in greater detail about **Elias** <note>Referring to *Elias Artista*, a legendary figure in alchemy predicted by Paracelsus who would appear at the end of days to reveal the ultimate secrets of nature.</note> than this Felgenhauer; he described him as being very near and coming soon, and wrote much and very specifically of his deeds—including how the "fleshly Christ" of the Christian churches would be completely cut away, as can be read in his little book, *The Postilion* <note>original: *Postillon*. A 1620 work by Felgenhauer regarding religious and natural prophecy.</note>. Regarding **Salt**, he also says: when Elias comes, he will tell you more about Salt.

The *Golden Chain of Homer* <note>original: *Aurea catena Homeri*. An influential 18th-century alchemical text that describes the circulation of the "World Spirit" through the kingdoms of nature.</note> says on page 6: The Philosophers have directed us to where the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: *Spiritus Mundi*. The universal life-force or vital breath that alchemists believed could be extracted from the atmosphere.</note> is found concentrated in its greatest, best, and healthiest form. This points quite tangibly to Air and Salt, for nowhere else can—

### Saltpeter, Tartar, and Air are the Principles of the Stone.

Where can one find the **World-Spirit** <note>original: *Weltgeist*; the vital, life-giving essence that alchemists believed permeated the universe and could be "captured" to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> more concentrated than in Salt? For the sake of brevity, I will only list the names of the books or the authors without indicating the page numbers, especially since these do not always match in different editions, and moreover, these **dicta** <note>original: *Dicta*; authoritative sayings or pronouncements.</note> are already well-known to lovers of chemical writings.

> **Jean d’Espagne** <note>A 17th-century French alchemist known for his work *The Garden of Hermes*.</note> says: "Seek a matter in which the **World-Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus Mundi*.</note> is most present and easiest to obtain." That is to say: seek a Salt, for without it one cannot have the World-Spirit. "If you cannot find such in the metallic kingdom, go to the minerals. If these will not help you either, go to **Saturn** <note>original: *Saturnus*. In standard alchemy, this refers to Lead, but the author here provides a specific substitute code.</note>, who will give the order to **Vulcan** <note>original: *Vulkano*. Usually the god of fire, representing the heat of the furnace.</note> to grant your request."

In metals and minerals, there is nothing to be done. But the **Saturn** of the Sages is **Saltpeter**, and **Vulcan** is **Tartar**, which is made capable by Saturn (or Saltpeter) to attract the World-Spirit; and in this way, Saturn gives the order to Vulcan.

->§. 3.<-

> ***Aurea Catena*** <note>Refers to the *Aurea Catena Homeri* (The Golden Chain of Homer), a highly influential book on the "chain of nature" and the recycling of elements.</note>: "Salt is the Mother of Attraction and desires the Man. Salt and Saltpeter forge everything in the world." **Tartar** is the salt, or the Mother of Attraction, and she desires the Man, which is the **Saltpeter**; then these two forge everything.

> ***Soliloquium***: "He who works in this Art without Salt is like one who wishes to shoot a bow without a string."

> **Philaletha, *Magia Adamica*** <note>A work by Thomas Vaughan (writing as Eugenius Philalethes), an English alchemist and mystic.</note>: "In Nature there is a singular spirit that joins itself to matter," namely to Salt. "There is a chain in Nature through which the higher and invisible natures, or spiritual essences, descend and dwell within matter, ennobling and multiplying it." **Salt** is the chain, for Salt is also in the air, and one salt attracts the other when it is made ready for it. "This beautiful matter..."

Namely, the superior spiritual matter of which he speaks on page 101, "submits itself to none but love," which points to those identical natures that attract one another. "It cannot be captured by force. "Now you must consider for yourselves where Nature offers the seed," namely in the Salt. "Yet many are so foolish that they do not know how they should work."

### §. 4.

Likewise: "The substance which we first take into our hands is mineral," that is Saltpeter, "of the kinship of **Mercury**," as the Air, "and is a child of **Saturn**." <note>As established on the previous page, the author uses "Saturn" as a code for Saltpeter and "Mercury" as a code for the Air or the spirit within it.</note> Because Saltpeter, as the Saturn of the Sages, causes the Air to be attracted. "It is a unique body in the earth that is very closely related to Mercury and is of Saturn’s lineage." This is all referring to Saltpeter.

The three parts of the Stone—namely Saltpeter, Tartar, and Air—are compared to the three kingdoms: Tartar is compared to the **vegetable kingdom**, because it comes from wine, which belongs among the vegetables via the grapevine; Air to the **animal kingdom**, because humans and animals live by the air; and thus Saltpeter is a likeness of the **mineral kingdom**. -peter <note>Completing the word "Saltpeter" from the previous page.</note> is thus the mineral substance which must be taken in hand first, because the Tartar cannot attract the air without it. According to these three parts, they also imagine three stones: the mineral, the vegetable, and the animal stone; yet it is always only one and the very same.

> Likewise: "One nature delights in another, one overcomes another, one rules another. Nature is not improved except within its own nature."

Through all such sayings, only homogeneity <note>the state of being of the same kind or nature.</note> is indicated, and that one must take a salt in order to attract the air. Since the salt actually attracts the air, it must also be of the same nature as the air, for "like only goes into its like."

> Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist who proposed that a vital "food of life" was hidden in the air.</note> says: "In the air there is a hidden food of life, which by night is called dew, and by day a thinned water," namely the air; "whose invisible spirit, when it is coagulated <note>turned from a gas or liquid into a solid.</note>, is better than the whole earth."

For when the **World-Spirit** <note>original: *Weltgeist*; the universal life force.</note> is properly dried and coagulated upon the salt, then the Stone is in its perfection. "O you holy and wonderful Nature, you who do not let your disciples go astray!"

->§. 5.<-

> Gloria Mundi <note>A famous 17th-century alchemical treatise titled "The Glory of the World."</note>: "The Salt is the fire and the dryness, which is actually the Tartar. Furthermore: "If you wish to become rich, prepare it so that it becomes sweet." The matter brought to completion in the **preliminary work** <note>original: *Vorarbeit*; the initial purification stages of the alchemical process.</note> is also called the **Moon**, which then becomes sweet in the **follow-up work** <note>original: *Nacharbeit*; the secondary stage of refinement and maturation.</note>. "Salt is the soul of the earth, it coagulates <note>turns from a liquid/gas into a solid.</note> all things, and is called **Rebis**." **Rebis** <note>from the Latin *res bina*, meaning a "double thing" or a "thing of two."</note> means a thing made of two, and that is **Tartar** and **Saltpeter**. Because they now coagulate, that which they coagulate must be the third thing, namely the **Air**; and so all three parts of the Stone are expressed very beautifully in these speeches. These are also further indicated in the following words: "It is one Stone, a Salt <note>original: *Sal*</note>, and a Body <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, and is the key to the whole Art. "These two saline substances are Sulfur and the Radical Moisture <note>original: *Humidum radicale*; the essential, life-sustaining fluid of a substance.</note>, for the Tartar is *Sulphur* or Sulfur, which holds the dry fire, and the Saltpeter is the Radical Moisture." "All of this is born in the most subtle earth by the wind," <note>A reference to the *Emerald Tablet* of Hermes Trismegistus: "The wind carried it in its belly."</note> that is, the salt is ground into a delicate flour after the smelting of the preparation, during which grinding it draws the air to itself and is then quickly sealed away; thus the thing is born, "and so it becomes a **Stone of the Philosophers**. This is the single thing from which everything becomes, and in it are all four elements."

This is a beautiful description, in which there is also wisdom, and no seduction into false matter. > Raymundus: "The earth is pure in the <term>Center</term> <note>original: *Centro*</note>, and cannot be consumed in the fire. In the <term>Center</term> <note>original: *Centro*</note> of the earth is a virgin earth," namely <term>Salt</term>, "the true <term>Element</term>, through which Nature was created, from which you shall understand what you must take."

All physical things have at their inner core a <term>Salt</term>, which remains in the ash and does not burn away with the coarse material in the fire, for it can be leached out of the ash. It is thus suggested by these words that one must take a <term>Salt</term> which takes the place of Earth in the Stone. And this he calls a virgin earth.

The <term>Salt</term> also truly persists in fire according to its nature when it is placed in the <term>crucible</term> <note>original: *Schmelztiegel*; a vessel used for melting substances at very high temperatures.</note>. Since water prevents fire from consuming a thing, it follows that the true <term>Element</term> of Water, or the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: *Grundfeuchtigkeit*</note>, must also be present within the <term>Salt</term>; this is especially found in <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>original: *Salpeter*; potassium nitrate, often called "Niter" in alchemical texts.</note>, which is also called the <term>radical moisture</term> <note>original: *Humidum radicale*; the essential, life-sustaining fluid of a substance.</note> in the cited passage from the <term>Glory of the World</term> <note>original: *Gloria Mundi*; a famous 16th-century alchemical text.</note>. All elements are in the <term>Salt</term> anyway, and none can exist without the others; and because it exists so preeminently in the <term>Salt</term> compared to other things, it lasts longer in the fire than other things, and therefore the <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> must consist of <term>Salt</term>. The earth and the sea together make up the sphere, and together they are the single <term>Subject</term> <note>The underlying material or "matter" upon which a process acts.</note> upon which the higher natures of the heavens act. In the same way, <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: *Tartar*; a salt found in wine barrels, used here to represent the terrestrial principle.</note> stands in place of the Earth, and <term>Saltpeter</term> stands in place of the sea, and both must be together if they are to form the <term>Subject</term> upon which the <term>World Spirit</term> <note>original: *Weltgeist*; the vital, animating force of the universe often equated with the "Air" or "Mercury" of the philosophers.</note> acts to create the <term>Philosophers' Stone</term>. See, therefore, I will [place] the <term>Philosophers' Stone</term> into the harmony of the whole...

->§. 7.<-

But there is still a mystery regarding Salt in view of its fire-resistant <note>original: *feuerbeständigen*; in alchemy, this refers to "fixity," the ability of a substance to remain stable and not volatilize or burn away when heated.</note> nature, of which this salt is only an image. Now, there is yet another corporeal essence in the coarse earth and in the bodies of humans, which is shining and transparent, and yet is nonetheless the temporal essence of the earth and of our bodies. When Babel <note>In this context, "Babel" represents the state of worldly confusion, corruption, or the fallen church, common in the mystical writings of Jacob Böhme and his followers.</note> has fallen, then the Kingdom of the Messiah shall come, which lasts thirty years. When these years are at an end, the satanic nature—which until then remains above the earth but is held captive—will suddenly and in a moment be driven into and enclosed within the interior of the earth. In this entry, all coarse corporeality of the earth and of human bodies is consumed, for it is the fire of the Last Judgment through which everything on earth must pass. In this fire, the true corporeality of the earth and of human bodies persists, and only the coarse essence remains in the fire; and that is then the true virgin earth or corporeality, of whose persistence in the fire the fire-resistance of Salt is an image and outward testimony. When the Philosopher's Stone <note>original: *Stein der Weisen*</note> is completed, its Salt is first truly placed into the fire-resistant state, and it transforms all lesser metals into the fire-resistant gold-grade—into the highest degree of metallic nature—and that is the true image and the essential testimony in outward nature of the restoration and restitution of all things fallen under the curse to their first state of perfection. Therefore, the Philosopher's Stone and its Salt is the most perfect and essential image in all of nature of Jesus Christ, the Savior and Restorer of all things.

> "At first she shall rule over me," for at first the Air rules with its moisture, "but afterward I shall rule over her, because I have then become the pursuer of my mother, once I have learned to flee from her. Yet she nourishes me like a pious mother until I have come to perfection." <note>This allegory describes the alchemical process: initially, the liquid (moisture/air) dominates the solid matter, but as the work progresses, the solid matter "captures" the spirit and becomes the dominant force.</note>

In the preliminary work, moisture still holds great power; however, in the subsequent work, dryness increases steadily. The Salt swallows the Air more and more, through which it becomes ever higher and more volatile, and it is continually nourished by the Air until it is complete.

In themselves, however, the elements in the lower regions are also active. But no generation can occur from heaven, as the male, alone, nor from the elements, or the female, alone, but only in the union of both. # *Saltpeter*, *Tartar*, and Air are the *Principles* of the Stone.

"...one and the same **substance**. Thus, when it is increased and strengthened by this similarity of **substance**, it triumphs over the corruptible nature by which it was previously suppressed." This means that when the power lying within the salt is strengthened by the air, it triumphs over the sharp, biting nature of the salt and makes it sweet. It is exactly the same within us humans and in all nature on earth: when our weakened power for good is strengthened by the holy being of God, our sick nature is healed and triumphs over the corruptible essence by which it has been suppressed until now. And so, the preparation of the Stone—especially in the subsequent work <note>In alchemy, the "subsequent work" or *post-work* refers to the later stages of refinement after the initial matter has been prepared.</note>—is a fundamental image of the renewal of all things through Jesus Christ. God, acting as nature, makes the Stone Himself and moves the matter from the curse into the blessing. In this way, He shows us how He intends to work within us and all creation. Our God and father Adam himself described and left behind for us the preparation of the Philosopher's Stone, as we shall see in the following; and with it, he has placed a visible example before our eyes of the possibility of how God can renew all things again and restore them from the curse into the blessing, so that we might believe firmly in Him and trust and build upon His promises. To this end, the revelation of the Stone is now occurring at the end of times, and it has happened for our benefit—we who are to inherit His kingdom on earth in the last days.

# **Saltpeter**, **Tartar**, and **Air** are the **Principles** of the Stone.

...through which the matter is made capable of attracting the air. "The stars are the hammers of this smith," namely the air, in which the celestial powers reside, "and the wind is the bellows," because the celestial powers arrive with the air. This is how things proceed within the Stone, and in exactly the same way, the heavens and all the stars work upon the earth and produce all things. Therefore, the Philosopher's Stone is also a teacher to us, instructing us how all things on earth are brought about by the starry heavens, and how heaven creates everything.

Likewise: "In the center of the earth there also lies a hidden sun," namely the salt, which is the sun and also the gold of the sages; "when this is violently attacked by its relative, the wind, it exercises its fiery art in its fiery workshop, under Mars in the center of the earth, and breaks forth from the subterranean caverns." With this, we are directed toward the salt, into which the air is meant to enter.

Likewise: "Our water does not wet the hands," for it is the air, "and it is from the nature of the body that has been **calcined** <term>calcination: the process of heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder or "ash," used in alchemy to purify a substance of its "gross" or physical impurities</term>, and from the nature of the spirit departing from it, and it is the life of all things that are in the whole world." The salt is, so to speak, consumed by the **world-spirit** <note>original: *Weltgeist*. Also known as the *Spiritus Mundi*, this was believed to be a universal life-force or "breath" that permeated all matter.</note>, which is the **calcination** of the sages, and also their **solution** <note>original: *Solution*. In alchemy, "solution" refers to the dissolving of a solid into a liquid state, often symbolizing the breaking down of the ego or the physical form.</note>, through which the inner power of the salt is dissolved and unites with the world-spirit. The entire unlocked **mass** <note>original: *Massa*</note> is then also called their "water," as will follow hereafter.

Likewise: "A subtle **saltpeter** <term>saltpeter: or niter; a mineral salt used as a primary active agent in alchemical recipes, often associated with the "celestial fire"</term> makes the earth," that is, the **tartar** <term>tartar: potassium bitartrate; a sediment from wine casks used as a source of "fixed" or stable salts</term>, "fruitful, for it is the life of the earth," because the tartar attracts no air without the saltpeter. "The artist should likewise know how to moisten his matter with such a dew, as much as the weight of nature requires," for it must receive very little air. <meta>This page continues the technical and spiritual explanation of the alchemical process, focusing on the specific "principles" (fundamental ingredients) required to create the Philosopher's Stone.
