# The Preparation of the Stone.

“...is torn apart and becomes earth, which happens in forty days.” Here the seven <term>rotations</term> <note>original: "Rotationes"; these are cycles of heating and cooling used to refine the alchemical matter.</note>, and also the forty days of each, are clearly indicated.

**Pandulphus** <note>A legendary figure from the "Turba Philosophorum," a famous early alchemical text.</note>: “One should note that the Philosopher calls the fermented stone the 'white world,'” which is the completed <term>Preliminary Work</term> <note>original: "Vorarbeit"; the initial phase of purifying the raw materials.</note>. “Afterward, one should moisten the body seven times. The entire amount of water one needs for the whole work is divided into three parts. With the ore, one sets one-third aside for <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: "Putrefaction"; the stage of decomposition or "death" of the matter, usually turning it black.</note>,” which constitutes the entire Preliminary Work. “The other two-thirds are sufficient for nourishment when they are poured over it seven times.” The other two parts thus belong to the white and red <term>tincture</term> <note>The essence capable of transforming metals; "white" for silver and "red" for gold.</note> in the <term>Finishing Work</term> <note>original: "Nacharbeit"; the final stages of the process.</note>, and so we see that they also use the triple number of the Stone in this manner.

**Theophilus** <note>Another philosophical authority cited in alchemical traditions regarding technical proportions.</note>: “Divide the poison <note>In alchemy, "poison" often refers to the volatile or raw state of the mercury or the starting material before it is "fixed."</note> into two equal parts,” for the Preliminary and Finishing Work. “Melt the ore with one part; grind it with the other. There should be two parts of the <term>elixir</term> <note>The refined substance that acts as a medicine for metals.</note> to seven,” for the white and red tincture, “or three parts to one,” for the three repetitions in the single Preliminary Work, or even three parts of the triple water to the one part of <term>ferment</term> <note>The substance added to the base matter to "leaven" it and direct its transformation.</note>.

“However, one should maintain that the Philosophers command the water to be divided into two parts to show that one should moisten the elixir after <term>fermentation</term> with just as much water as it required during fermentation,” that is, the glass vessel should have just as large an air space in the Preliminary Work as in the Finishing Work. (Though some conclude otherwise against this and teach only three parts in number, since only the three rotations of the Preliminary Work are meant.) “And that it must afterward be made moist and nourished through seven alternations, in whatever parts or sizes the ore was dissolved,” namely, with three parts of air in the vessel. “When this indicated proportion has been added to the elixir through seven alternations, then the elixir is ripe and ready to <term>tinge</term> <note>To "tinge" is the final act of the Stone, where it instantly transforms a base metal into gold or silver.</note>. But when the vessel...

->§ 13.<-

All true philosophers agree that the Stone is
formed in nine months, just like a child in the mother's womb, and
is born into the world in the tenth month; however, these nine months
take place in both the <term>Preliminary Work</term> <note>original: "Vorarbeit"; the initial purification phase.</note> and the <term>Finishing Work</term> <note>original: "Nacharbeit"; the final perfection of the substance.</note>.
Senior <note>Senior Zadith (Zadith ben Hamuel), a 10th-century Arabian alchemist whose work "The Silvery Water and the Earthy Star" was a major influence on later European alchemy.</note> assigns to this formation seven days, ten days, seventeen days, twenty-four
days, and forty days, in various sections, within which the
seed would be coagulated, and in which the limbs and nerves
would be formed. The forty days are the duration of one rotation in
the Finishing Work. However, if they are taken solely according to <term>numerology</term> <note>original: "Zahlenlehre"; the symbolic and mystical study of numbers.</note>;
then they are also the forty weeks in the Preliminary Work, as
well as the forty weeks in the Finishing Work. All these numbers
taken together, however, make ninety-eight, which encompasses the time of
one rotation in the Preliminary Work. Each rotation
must have its own complete formation, and all taken together
also constitute a whole formation; and so the numbers forty,
ninety, and also the whole are contained within this time of formation.
Senior says: “From the fortieth day it begins to be
nourished,” for at the end of a forty-day period in the
Finishing Work, one gives it fresh nourishment, and likewise at the end of the
forty weeks of the Preliminary Work. Further: “The soul begins from the
blood and becomes hard, and the child grows, and gradually
becomes hard, and gains a form. And when the nine months are
fulfilled, it is torn away from where it was previously nourished,”

The seven days are also the seven <term>rotations</term> <note>original: "Rotationen"; complete cycles of the alchemical process.</note>, the ten days are the ten <term>eagles</term> <note>original: "Adler"; an alchemical symbol usually referring to the process of sublimation or the strength of the volatile spirit.</note>, the seventeen days are seven and ten taken together, and the twenty-four days are the doubled number twelve, which lies hidden within the matter in both the Finishing Work and the Preliminary Work. The two waters are the Preliminary and Finishing Work, and the forty days are the time of one rotation in the Finishing Work, and then they also represent the whole with forty weeks.

# The Preparation of the Stone.

"...which no other precedes, and there are three earths, as *Senior* <note>Senior Zadith (Zadith ben Hamuel), a highly respected 10th-century alchemist often quoted in later Hermetic texts.</note> says, there are three compositions, three <term>rotations</term> <note>original: "Rotationes"; cyclic stages of heating and cooling.</note> in the preliminary work; and the time until the white <term>tincture</term> <note>The stage of the "White Work" or Albedo, which supposedly turns metals into silver.</note> and until the red tincture <note>The final "Red Work" or Rubedo, which produces the Stone capable of making gold.</note> are, with the preliminary work, also three.

With two gums she fixes the volatile in the depths," namely with <term>tartar</term> <note>original: "Tartar"; potassium bitartrate, a byproduct of winemaking used as a reagent.</note> and <term>saltpeter</term> <note>original: "Salpeter"; potassium nitrate, used as a powerful oxidizing agent.</note>, and in both the preliminary and finishing work. "In three hours she binds three strong ones and makes the end," in the three rotations and in the three times of the preliminary work, white, and red tincture, wherein the three parts of air are bound in the glass. "*Mary* binds the light of the dew in three hours. The daughter of *Pluto*," <note>original: "Tochter Plutonis"; a poetic reference to the dark, "underworld" state of the base matter.</note> the matter, "makes the obligation of love, and rejoices over the roasted seed," over the *coagulated* air, "which through the three and with them have been united together."

God has made all things in number, measure, and weight. <note>A reference to the Biblical Book of Wisdom 11:20, a foundational concept for alchemists who believed chemistry mirrored the divine creation of the universe.</note> Because the Stone of the Wise is meant to be, and indeed is, the most perfect image of the Godhead, the whole world, and the whole Church; how then should it not have its numbers, measures, and divisions? # The Preparation of the Stone.

...let alone a useful and good thing; rather, it is God alone who begets and makes everything, and God is Nature—namely, the external God is external Nature. Therefore, Nature must necessarily beget the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: "Stein der Weisen"; the supreme object of alchemy, believed to facilitate spiritual perfection and the transmutation of metals.</note>. And because nothing can be begotten without two, such as man and woman, so also must two main subjects be present, which are the <term>World Spirit</term> <note>original: "Weltgeist"; the animating, volatile essence that permeates the universe.</note> and the Salt, the Above and the Below. Now, if one were to place a procreative power into a <term>coal fire</term> <note>original: "Kohlfeuer"; here used as a critique of "soulless" laboratory chemistry that relies on artificial heat rather than the natural, internal heat of the universe.</note>, what would come of it? Does Nature also require a coal fire for its procreation? Terrible blindness!

But because every birthing action has its time in which it is accomplished, as is seen in all things, so also must the Philosopher's Stone have its precisely measured time. Is it not then a great wonder that it, like man, has forty weeks or nine months, and also forty days in the <term>rotation</term> <note>original: "Rotation"; a stage in the alchemical process where the substance is circulated or refined repeatedly.</note> in the final work, wherein the right life and spirit are brought into the Stone, and indeed another forty weeks in the entire subsequent work? Where is there a greater wonder among all created things than man? Is man not the actual true image of God? Therefore, let the Stone, which is created in the number of man, also be a wonder to me, and let it be for me the image and likeness of the Essence of all Essences, and of our Holy God and Father Jesus Christ! Let it be for me the herald who announces to us with its revelation that <term>Jehovah</term> <note>original: "Jehova"; the personal name of God in the Hebrew Bible.</note> will reveal His holy arm before all flesh, and that we shall all come to know Him, both small and great. Yes, come, Lord Jesus, Amen.

### The Preparation of the Stone.

Here the days of both <term>rotations</term> <note>original: "Rotationen"; refers to the repeated cycles of heating, evaporation, and condensation intended to purify the matter.</note> in the first and second work are quite clearly indicated, yet they are wisely hidden from anyone who has not walked the path of the teachings of wisdom through comparisons. The first work is compared here to the Red, as to <term>Copper</term> <note>original: "Kupfer"; in alchemy, copper is often associated with the planet Venus, but here it represents a state of raw, unrefined energy.</note>, because the properties of nature have more to struggle there than in the second work. This struggle is then compared to <term>Mars</term> <note>original: "Mars"; the alchemical symbol for iron and warfare, representing a violent or energetic reaction.</note>, for the <term>saltpeter</term> <note>original: "Salpeter"; a volatile mineral salt considered a key catalyst in many early modern chemical processes.</note> is not yet overcome and swallowed up there. The second work, however, is compared to the White, as the color of heaven. The forty days precede the ninety days, according to the manner of the teachings of wisdom, which is to put the last first where it is necessary. This was again a veil, for the inexperienced person might understand "the Red" to mean the red <term>tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur"; the final, concentrated essence capable of transforming metals or healing the body.</note> at the end, and thus he would be excluded from this garden of paradise, with the <term>Cherub</term> <note>A reference to the biblical guardian of Eden; here, it symbolizes the intellectual barriers that prevent the unworthy from understanding the secret process.</note> standing before it.

> Morienus: <note>Morienus Romanus was a legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist said to have taught the secrets of the Stone to the King of Egypt.</note> “You should know that for this masterpiece one must have two creations, or two preparations, the first and second work, which are both so connected to one another that when one of them is completed, the other begins. Its first disposition is exactly the same as the other.” This is a testimony that the proportion of the matter relative to the vessel must be the same in the first and second work, and that consequently, in every <term>fermentation</term> <note>original: "Fermentation"; the stage where the Stone is given its transformative power by adding a "ferment," much like leaven is added to dough.</note>, one must take away as much as one adds with the ferment.

> Also: “There are three that fulfill and constitute our <term>Elixir</term>. The first joins,” in the first work, “the other two make up the Medicine,” the white and red tincture in the second work. This is again a testimony that the number three is also understood in this way. “But when this medicine is resolved again, and directed throughout as before, then there shall be no end to its tincture.” These are only the openings in the follow-up work, for these are the <term>multiplication</term> <note>original: "Multiplication"; the process of increasing the quantity and potency of the finished Stone so that a small amount can transform a vast quantity of base metal.</note> of the stone, which however must not be pushed beyond the <term>number seven</term> <note>original: "Siebenzahl"; the number seven often represents a limit of natural perfection or a complete cycle in alchemical philosophy.</note>. Such discourses were

### The Preparation of the Stone.

This is also a veil <note>The original German "Brille" literally means "spectacles" or "glasses," but here functions as an idiom for an illusion or obstacle placed before the unworthy.</note> for the **sophists** <note>In alchemical literature, "sophists" refers to false practitioners or "puffers" who lack true philosophical understanding.</note>, who have imagined that the **Stone** can always be increased <note>original: "vermehret"; refers to "multiplication," the stage where the Stone's power to transform metals is increased.</note> after it has been completely finished. This is not so; for example, at the end of the seventh **rotation** <term>rotation</term> <note>original: "Rotation"; a repeated cycle of distillation or heating intended to purify the matter.</note>, one would have to take out the whole mass and give it a body of **mercury** <note>original: "Quecksilber"; here referring to alchemical quicksilver used as a vehicle for the concentrated essence.</note> over the fire by letting both run together, with which one could then **tincture** <term>tincture</term> <note>The act of transforming a base metal into gold or silver using the finished Stone.</note>; otherwise, it would surely spoil the air. One could not enclose it again, because it would thereby receive an eighth rotation, from which it would necessarily perish anyway. Whatever one wished to use for medicine, however, must be put into wine.

Also: "With the first water, **Sun and Moon**," <note>Alchemical code for Gold and Silver.</note> **tartar** and **saltpeter**, "are **calcined** <term>calcination</term> <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "ash."</note>, so that the bodies are opened, so that the second water," in the second work, "can enter all the better. The **ferment** is the fourth part of our ore." The matter occupies the fourth part of the space inside the glass.

**Arnoldus:** <note>Likely referring to Arnold of Villanova (c. 1240–1311), a famous physician and alchemist.</note> "There are three minerals from which our Stone is extracted, and God has created three noble **Mercuries**. One is mineral," **saltpeter**, "the second is vegetable and is in wine," **tartar**, "and the third is animal," **air**. "From these three, the living **Mercury** is enticed, which the wise seek, and which contains the four elements within itself." A clear testimony of the materials of the Stone.

Also: "The water of **Mercury**, with which we **dissolve** <note>original: "solviren"; the process of breaking down a solid into a liquid state.</note>, shall be pure, and it shall only be made from the **purified dragon**," **tartar**. "But the dragon is purified when one **elevates** <note>original: "eleviret"; to sublimate or raise the substance through heat.</note> it three times and then makes it living." These are the three rotations in the first work, and also the first work together with the two tinctures in the last work.

### The Preparation of the Stone.

“Purification is necessary, which consists in the addition of **sulfur** <note>In this context, the author identifies sulfur with tartar.</note> little by little, according to the number of **Eagles** <term>Eagles</term> <note>original: "Adler"; an alchemical term for the repeated distillations or sublimations of mercury. Seven to ten "Eagles" were typically required to purify the metallic spirit.</note>; then it will be purified from the ground up.” This is a testimony that during the **rotations** <term>rotation</term> <note>A repeated cycle of heating and cooling or distilling.</note> of the first work, one must add **tartar** as a **ferment**, for the sulfur is tartar.

Also: Regarding the Eagles, he says: “Know, however, that from seven to ten times, the **mercury** is made better and better, and more effective, because in each preparation it is strengthened by our true sulfur. But if one exceeds the number of preparations, it will become too fiery and, instead of dissolving the body, it will **coagulate** <term>coagulation</term> itself” in moisture.

From seven to ten is three, and these three are the three repetitions in the first work where one must add sulfur or tartar. Regarding the progress of the work, he says:

“You must boil this stone with that very same water, in the very same proportion, and with the very same **regimen** <term>regimen</term> <note>The specific management of the heat and timing during the alchemical process.</note>.” This is a proof that one must always maintain the proportion of the matter to the vessel, and also add the same amount of ferment each time.

Also: “Our stone is made from a single root and two mercurial substances. First is the body, which is **Sun**,” <note>original: "Sol"; usually gold, but here the author identifies it as tartar.</note> tartar; “secondly is the water of **Mercury**,” the air; “and thirdly, within such, alongside its mercuriality, is a spiritual seed of sulfur, which is the secret fire,” **saltpeter** <note>Potassium nitrate, a key ingredient in many "dry" alchemical paths.</note>. “The body is the dead earth, which does not increase without the heavenly power. The spirit is the soul of our air, or the **chameleon** <term>chameleon</term> <note>A substance that changes appearance or state, often referring to the versatile solvent or the volatile spirit that captures the influences of the air.</note>, which is also of a twofold composition,” made of tartar and saltpeter, which attract the air, “and yet is distinctly made into one. The soul is the bond of mercury, without which our fire can never be seen,” for without the saltpeter, no air enters the matter. “She is the true **magical steel of the wise**,” <note>original: "magische Stahl der Weisen"; a metaphor for the catalyst—like steel striking flint—that ignites the internal fire of the matter.</note> because the saltpeter must strike the fire within the matter.

### The Preparation of the Stone.

Also: "You should know for certain that although the **mercury** <term>mercury</term> <note>In alchemical practice, this refers to the 'spirit' or solvent, often derived from specialized salts rather than common quicksilver.</note> 'eats up' the **gold**, the **tartar**, it does not happen in the way that the so-called philosophers imagine. For if you do not cook it with the proper degree of fire"—that is, if the proportion of the **tartar** and **saltpeter** <term>saltpeter</term> <note>Potassium nitrate, used here as an oxidizing agent in the initial melt.</note> is not correct from the beginning—"then you will take out that very same gold again in its original condition after the passing of a year, if you do not set it together correctly with the mercury, even if you have already combined them." 

This means that if one uses too much saltpeter in the first fusion on the coal fire, it later attracts too much air, making the tartar-ferment too weak to resist the moisture. The air becomes too thick and physical, and thus the subtle spirit of the air cannot unite with the subtle power of the tartar. The tartar-ferment remains by itself and does not unite with the matter—which has been 'animated' by the air—into a single spirit and essence, even though a composition of both has occurred; for it was not made correctly. The proper proportioning at the beginning, together with the tartar-ferment, is the **Seal of Hermes** <note>original: "Sigillum Hermetis"; a term for an airtight seal, but here used metaphorically for the perfect chemical ratio that 'locks' the process in place.</note>. Whoever does not do this correctly has no warrant or seal that they will attain anything.

### §. 19.

**Dornäus** <note>Gerard Dorn (c. 1530–1584), a Belgian philosopher and one of the most important commentators on Paracelsus.</note>: "Make a **circle** out of man and woman"—that is, melt the tartar and saltpeter together into one. "From the circle make a **square**"—that is, let the two melted substances attract air, then there are three, and then give them the tartar-ferment, so it becomes a square of four. "From the square make a **triangle**"—that is, perform the three repetitions in the preliminary work: "and finally a circle again, and then you have the **Stone**"—that is, let it remain a single thing in the final work, and unite spirit and matter into one, and then you have the **Stone** <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosophers' Stone, the ultimate goal of the alchemy, capable of transmuting metals and perfecting human health.</note>.

Trithemius expresses this matter thus:

> „Every miraculous 
> „operation that remains within the bounds of nature rises 
> „from **Unity** through the **Number Two** into the **Number Three**, and is 
> „brought from the **Number Four** through the **Number Three** back into **Unity**.

To this Number Three, one must add the **tartar-ferment**, and so one has the Number Four. With this Number Four, one goes back into Unity through the Number Three of the three rotations in the "first work"—namely, into the "after-work," wherein only a single material remains without any further addition. Thus, one rejects the Number Two of the tartar and saltpeter—from which, in themselves, the Stone cannot be made—but instead one seeks the Air through them, thus obtaining the Number Three; and this is brought back into Unity through the tartar-ferment (as the Number Four) and bound together into one: then you have the Art.

See, thus the **Philosophers' Stone** is a subject of a thousandfold wise contemplations, and no **Adept** <note>original: "Adeptus"; a master who has successfully completed the Great Work.</note> has ever found it necessary to fill his writings with trifles. Meanwhile, some have done so nonetheless, and there is not a **dram** <note>original: "Quentlein"; a small historical unit of weight, about 3.7 grams.</note> of true wisdom in their scribblings. Therefore, the Stone shall judge them in its revelation as **wicked servants** <note>A biblical allusion to the Parable of the Talents (Matthew 25:26), where a servant is punished for failing to invest his master's money.</note>, who did not apply the pound entrusted to them for good, but in their wantonness for evil and the harm of their neighbor; and their "stone" shall save them on the Day of the Lord just as little as his "Christ" will save the **mouth-Christian** <note>original: "Maulchristen"; a derogatory term for a hypocrite who is Christian in name only, but not in deed or spirit.</note>. But that this Stone described here is the Judge, and the true Stone, shall be attested to it by all the testimonies of the **Adepts** <note>original: "Adeptorum"</note> cited in this book, who all—quite naturally and perfectly—un- <note>The text breaks off here; the catchword from the bottom of the page indicates the next word is "close" or "lock," forming "unfold/reveal" (aufschliessen).</note>

### §. 20.

Ripley: <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was one of England’s most famous alchemists, known for his work "The Compound of Alchymy."</note> „First, perform the rotation of your elements, and „above all things, first turn your Earth into Water, your Water „into Air, and your Air into Fire.„ These are three stages of the transformation of the elements, from the bottom up, by which the dissolution of matter in the three repetitions is intended; and now he says further:

> „In this way, turn your wheel back, and transform the Fire 
> „into Water, and the Air into Earth, otherwise you labor in vain. For 
> „thus the Stone is brought into equality, when one is made 
> „from the four opposing natures. After they have now 
> „been circulated three times,„ with three such rotations, „then 
> „your basis or foundation will be completely established. Do not yet 
> „open or move your glass, from beginning to end, for otherwise 
> „your work cannot be completed.„

The Earth is **tartar** <term>tartar</term> <note>Potassium bitartrate, the "fixed" salt</note> and the **saltpeter** <term>saltpeter</term> <note>Potassium nitrate, the "volatile" salt</note> is water. When, in the first preparation, tartar and saltpeter are melted together, the Earth is transformed into water. Then it attracts **Air**, and the water is transformed into Air. When further the **ferment** <note>An additive, often described as "philosophical gold," that initiates the final transformation</note> is added to it and the matter is enclosed in the vessel, the Air is transformed into Fire. Now it must proceed from above, and the superior must act upon the inferior, unite with it, and improve it; therefore, the contrary elements are united, such as Fire into Water, and Air into Earth. When this happens three times, only then is the basis or the threefold tartar-foundation laid, and the Stone is created; but now it must first be born again in the final work, and must first itself be **tinctured** <term>tincture</term> <note>In alchemy, to "tincture" means to transform or color a base metal into a noble one through a spiritual or chemical influence</note> by the spirit of the world; therefore the Sages say: **Our Stone does not tincture, unless it first be tinctured.**

# The Preparation of the Stone.

->§. 21.<-

All perfection lies in unity, and all imperfection lies in the division of the elements into four. In the Stone, this quaternary exists essentially, for **tartar** <term>tartar</term> <note>Potassium bitartrate, the "fixed" salt representing Earth.</note>, **saltpeter** <term>saltpeter</term> <note>Potassium nitrate, the "volatile" salt representing Water.</note>, **Air**, and for such, the **ferment** <note>The substance added to the alchemical base to "ripen" it into gold or medicine.</note> are four. These must be brought into one, so that the Stone rests in the number ten, just as from one, two, three, and four, through union, the number ten arises <note>This refers to the Pythagorean *Tetractys* (1+2+3+4=10), a mystical symbol representing the completion of the universe.</note>. If one gives ten to the end of the first rotation; then a hundred come to the second, and a thousand to the third. These are no empty words, but essence and truth, for at the end of a rotation the power must have increased tenfold against what it was in the beginning, merely on the basis of unity, as the aforementioned four parts come together in power into one. If this tenfold perfection from the first rotation is now placed into the second; it must indeed also increase tenfold in power, and that is a hundred. If this is placed again into the third rotation, it has at its end a thousand. This is the highest number; therefore, the preliminary work must not have more than three rotations, and here the Stone must rest in the thousandth number, and in the subsequent work <note>German: *Nacharbeit*; the final refining stages of the alchemical process.</note> multiply itself in the thousandth number through the **seven degrees of nature's birth** <note>The seven classical alchemical stages: calcination, solution, separation, conjunction, putrefaction, coagulation, and cibation.</note>; so the first rotation in the second work brings a thousand times a thousand, as 1,000,000, and the second brings 1,000,000,000,000, and so forth, each time adding twice as many zeros.

The property of numbers therefore also testifies that this Stone described here is the true **Stone of the Wisdom of God**, and that it is the true image of the great All of all things. Because our Lord and God calls himself a stone, and has shared his own name with this miraculous creature, and calls it a stone; so it also has the same name, **Christ** <note>original: "Christus"</note>, which means an Anointed one <note>A reference to the alchemical concept of the *Lapis-Christus* parallel, where the Stone’s perfection mirrors the divinity of Christ.</note>, for it is with the—

# The Seventh Chapter.

## The Emerald Tablet of Hermes.

->§. 1.<-

The <term>Emerald Tablet of Hermes</term> <note>original: "Tabula Hermetis"; the Smaragdine Tablet, a foundational text of Hermetic philosophy and alchemy.</note> was written by the finger of God, just as He also wrote the two Testaments <note>The Old and New Testaments of the Bible.</note>, which shall become clear here. It is a brief summary of the entire work of the Stone, of the whole creation, and of the whole Church of God on Earth—three taken together as one. Therefore, it is said at the conclusion of the tablet: "And I am called <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>original: "Hermes Trisimegistus," meaning "Thrice-Greatest."</note> for this reason: because I possess the three parts of the wisdom of the whole world."

The teaching of the <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> <note>original: "Stein der Weisen"</note> is allegorical and figurative, much like the Word of God, and is not to be understood according to the literal letter. This is simultaneously a proof that it shares the same origin as the Word of God and comes from a single source. That it is figurative is testified by all the <term>sophists</term> <note>In an alchemical context, "sophists" refers to "puffers"—those who pursued literal gold-making without spiritual understanding.</note> who took metals, minerals, and other things literally, and sought the <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> within them but found nothing. Now, because <term>Elijah</term> <note>original: "Elias." In the tradition of Paracelsus and later alchemists, "Elias Artista" was a prophetic figure expected to appear in the end times to restore true knowledge of nature and grace.</note> must come before the Last Day and restore all teachings that have come from God back to their true sense—the sense in which our God wrote them—he must also restore the teachings of the <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term>. Therefore, this <term>Emerald Tablet</term> shall be presented here in its true sense. The Stone shares the same name as our God, and <term>Elijah</term> also shares the same name with our God, <term>Jesus Christ</term> <note>original: "Christo Jesu"</note>. # The Tablet of Hermes <note>original: "Tabula Hermetis"; the Emerald Tablet.</note>

## §. 2.

> “Truly, without falsehood, certain, and most true: that which is below is like that which is above, and that which is above is like that which is below, to accomplish the miracles of one single thing.”

There is an eternal nature in four eternal elements, and there is an external, temporal nature in four temporal elements. There is a nature of the upper world with the starry heaven, and there is a nature of the lower world. Nature stands everywhere in <term>four elements</term> <note>The classical elements: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth, which alchemists believed existed in both a physical and a spiritual/divine form.</note> and has the same divisions in its numbers. That which is below is like that which is above, and that which is above is like that which is below. God made it so for this purpose: to achieve the wonders of a Single One—the miraculous creations of human creatures by a single God, whom He created as beings in the preliminary work of this time. This miracle shall now become perfect upon Earth, as the Upper shall be united with the Lower, and the Eternal with the Temporal into one. Then the eternal God will live, work, and flourish in the temporal God <note>This likely refers to the "divine spark" within the world or the "divine man."</note>, the eternal nature in the external nature, and the inner man in the outer man; and there, one will see the wonders of a Single One.

At present, the perfection and goodness of the Eternal and the Upper are not in the Lower and the Temporal; otherwise, it would not be necessary for the Upper to first be united with the Lower in order to make the Lower perfect and to work miracles within it.

# The Seventh Chapter.
## The Tablet of Hermes.

->§. 1.<-

The *Tablet of Hermes* was written by the finger of God, just as He also wrote the two Testaments, which shall become clear here. It is a brief summary of the entire work of the <term>Stone</term> <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical "Great Work."</note>, of the whole creation, and of the whole Church of God on earth—three joined together into one. Therefore, it is said at the end of the tablet: "And I am called *Hermes Trismegistus* <note>original: "Hermes Trismegistus"; Greek for "Hermes the Thrice-Greatest," a legendary figure identified with the Egyptian god Thoth and the Greek god Hermes.</note>, because I possess the three parts of the wisdom of the whole world."

The teaching of the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> is allegorical and figurative, much like the Word of God, and is not to be understood according to the letter; this itself serves as proof that it shares the same origin as the Word of God and comes from the same Source. That it is figurative is attested by all the <term>sophists</term> <note>In alchemical literature, "sophists" are those who practice "false" or purely literal alchemy, focusing only on chemical recipes without spiritual understanding.</note> who took metals, minerals, and other things literally, seeking the Philosopher's Stone within them but finding nothing. Now, because <term>Elijah</term> <note>A reference to "Elias Artista" (Elijah the Artist), a prophetic figure expected by alchemists to appear in the end times to reveal the final secrets of nature and alchemy.</note> must come before the Last Day to restore all teachings that have come from God to their true sense—the sense in which our God wrote them—he must also restore the teachings of the Philosopher's Stone. For this reason, this *Tablet of Hermes* shall be presented here in its true sense. The Stone shares its name with our God, and Elijah also shares his name with our God, Jesus Christ. God reveals Himself, and He reveals Himself through that which is His name and what bears His name; therefore, He reveals Himself through Elijah, and Elijah must reveal both God Himself and His image in external nature, the Philosopher's Stone. In this way, God thought of many other natures of the world, which were to imagine into or enter into one another and act, and into which He also wished to enter with His eternal being, in order to create what was His primary purpose; and thus, various natures of the world arose for the bringing forth of things, just as there are various things in the Stone which, through their combined action, must produce that which is desired and sought from the Stone.

Just as all things have originated and are brought forth through this arrangement of the three primary natures of the world, and through their connecting union—where they must act upon each other, yet each in its own degree—so too does everything desired of the Stone originate through the arrangement of its three parts and through their connecting union. And all of this originates from the single **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"; the universal spirit or "World Soul" that acts as the intermediary between spirit and matter</note> or World Spirit, from which all lower things—and especially Salt and Tartar—derive in a more immediate degree.

<meta>This page continues the interpretation of the "Tablet of Hermes" (the Emerald Tablet), focusing on the specific lines concerning the parentage and nourishment of the alchemical Stone. ### Tablet of Hermes

<note>original: "Tabula Hermetis"; referring to the famous Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, a foundational text of alchemy.</note>

->§. 4.<-

**The Sun is its father, the Moon its mother, the wind has carried it in its belly, the earth is its nurse. This is the father of all perfection of the whole world. Its power is complete when it is turned into earth.**

In the Stone, the <term>Tartar</term> <note>a potassium bitartrate residue from wine casks, here used as a philosophical principle of fixity</note> is the Sun and the father, and the <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>original: "Salpeter"; used here as the volatile or "lunar" principle</note> is the Moon and the mother. They are called Father and Mother because they beget or attract the air. The wind, as the air, carries this spirit or Stone in its body. Its nurse is the Earth as the <term>Salt</term>, for therein it is strengthened in power and <term>virtue</term> <note>in this context, "virtue" refers to the inherent medicinal or transformative potency of a substance</note>, so that it is more powerful than it was when it was in the mere air; for by assuming a body, it becomes more substantial and higher, just as man is higher when he is a created being than he was as the mere floating power in nature from which he was made. This is the father of all perfection of the whole world. It is the most perfect being of all outward beings in the world thus far, because the elements within it are in equilibrium, which until now has been the case in no other thing on earth; therefore, it is an image of the future world, in which there is no more sickness, death, nor decay. Its power is complete when it is turned into earth—that is, when the spirit of the world transforms into one with the matter and becomes bodily within it. This is the matter with respect to the Stone, but the Stone is not yet the father of all perfection of the whole world; rather, that is God Himself, as we shall now see further.

Before creation, God was nothing other than the eternal nature in the entire realm that the world now occupies: but through the creation of man, the divine being wished to introduce Himself into a greater glory than existed before. To this end, the essence of God's eternal nature first formed itself into a human image, specifically in the center of the world, which is the Sun, and that was our...

### Tablet of Hermes

T 3
<meta>catchword: -ness (original: "keit", completing "Leiblichkeit")</meta>

...our father *Adam*, the personal God *Jesus Christus*. <note>The author uses a mystical framework where the "First Adam" and the "Last Adam" (Christ) are seen as a single divine prototype for humanity.</note> He carried the work of creation further down into the lower nature on earth, in which the procreation of mankind was to take place. He Himself participated in the creation, ruling and ordering it, while the general nature of the world carried out the making. Everything has come from God, and most especially mankind; therefore, He first had to be a human being Himself, so that mankind could come from God as if from a human being. He begat His children, and the universal nature of the world had to help form them; thus one human had to proceed from another, yet the nature of the world still had to create them through the cooperation of human nature. Through this, the nature of the world planted itself into mankind, and the essence of God became a human being within every person.

Through the act of creation, the eternal powers of God have stepped out from the spiritual and hidden realm into a physical and more manifest essence. When all of this is completed, then everything is perfect, and God is the father of all perfection of the whole world. <note>This is a direct commentary on the line from the Emerald Tablet: "Pater omnis telesmi totius mundi est hic" (The father of all perfection in the whole world is here).</note> There, the spiritual Stone—*Jesus Christus*—is perfect in all His members, for He, in His universal essence of the world, has been transformed into physical human *Kreaturen* <note>meaning "created beings," from the Latin *creatura*</note>, even though the essence of the world remains what it is outside of mankind; yet in man, it exists in greater power and glory than it does outside of him. Now the world is divided into the eternal and the temporal, and into the upper and the lower.

The eternal and upper realm is the father of this Stone of the human race, and the temporal and lower realm is the mother, in which the creation occurs. The wind—or the universal spirit and essence of God—carries him in its body and introduces itself into man during his formation. Through this, the essence of nature is, as it were, strengthened within the creaturely essence of man and hardened into a powerful, intelligible being; this takes place in the lower nature on earth. When this has happened, the divine essence is [transformed] into a spiritual, heavenly earth and physical-

It describes how the spiritual becomes manifest in the "lower nature" of the world through the process of human creation.</summary>

### Tablet of Hermes
<meta>catchword: from</meta>

...transformed into corporeality <note>original: *Leiblichkeit*</note>, and Jesus Christ—our father Adam—is the father of all perfections of the whole world, because all of this has come to be through him and out of him. This is the complete meaning of these words, and in this way God compares Himself to the **Philosopher’s Stone**; thus the mystery of God and the mystery of the Stone are grasped as one under the same words. Therefore, the **Philosopher’s Stone** is no object made from dead metals, minerals, or other things over a coal fire—for God does not compare Himself with such things—but rather with the life of the outer world, from which the Stone originates. The Sun rules and acts primarily upon the upper world, and the Moon is the ruler of the lower world. Through the upper and lower world everything is made; therefore, the Sun and Moon are also the father and the mother of it all.

In the Church, the Old Testament <note>original: "A." for *Altes Testament*</note> is the Sun and the father of the true doctrine in the <term>Revealed Christianity</term> <note>original: "O. C.", likely *Offenbartes Christenthum*, referring to an esoteric or "inner" understanding of the faith as opposed to the external church</note> and the general church; and the New Testament <note>original: "N." for *Neues Testament*</note> under the name of Jesus is the Moon and the mother. For the two Testaments are the father and mother of the true doctrine of wisdom, which is born out of them at the end, and to which the general church must bow and honor this father and mother. The Christian church is also the mother from which **Revealed Christianity** is born. The wind—namely, the Holy Spirit—has carried this Stone in its body <note>A reference to the Emerald Tablet: *Portavit eum ventus in ventre suo* (The wind carried it in its belly)</note>, and the earth of the Christian church is its nourisher, in which the spirit of revelation has long resided and from which it now emerges. Its power is complete when the believers of all nations have entered into the Christian church and doctrine, which is compared to the earth. This Stone, as the doctrine in the **Revealed Christianity**, is the father of all perfection, above all other doctrines on the entire earth.

->§ 5<-

You shall separate the earth from the fire, and the subtle from the coarse, quite gently and with great understanding. It ascends

# Tablet of Hermes

->from the earth up to heaven, and from heaven back to the earth, and it attains the power of the upper and the lower.<-

The fire of the stone is the air, and from it one shall obtain the Dryness, which is the earth, but one should not take the coarse moisture. Therefore, the subtle shall be separated from the coarse, and one shall obtain a subtle dry spirit, which shall be brought about quite pleasantly and with great understanding. It shall happen "pleasantly" because there must be a harmony of natures—of the materials and the spirit—so that they readily join together. And it shall happen with "great understanding" because one must fundamentally know what materials are required for the Stone. One must know that it consists of the Upper and the Lower, Air and Salt, and one must know what kind of salt it is. Even if one knows this, one still knows nothing. First, one must know what to do with it, which involves the proportion of the matter, what constitutes the preparatory and subsequent work, and what the <term>rotations</term> <note>These are the repeated cycles of distillation and condensation in an alchemical vessel, often called "circulations."</note> and their timing are. To know all of this certainly requires understanding, but even that is not yet the "great understanding," for that pertains to more and higher things than the **Philosopher’s Stone**.

It rises up from the earth toward heaven, and from heaven back to the earth, for the <term>Salt-Magnet</term> <note>original: *Salz-Magnet*; in alchemical theory, this is a substance believed to attract and "trap" the celestial or astral spirit from the air.</note> ascends toward the air, and the air descends toward the salt. And in the glass, the power is dissolved into <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: *Tartar*; while chemically potassium bitartrate, in this context it refers to a crystalline "salt of the earth" that serves as a base for the work.</note> and rises above itself, and the spirit of the air unites with it and enters into the salt, becoming one with it. Thus it attains the power of the Upper and the Lower, and the Upper unites with the Lower and transforms the Lower into its spiritual nature. This is how the **New Birth** <note>original: *neue Geburth*; a central concept in Christian Theosophy, suggesting that just as the Stone is refined, the human soul must be spiritually reborn.</note> of the Stone occurs, and in the very same way it must occur in man and in the Church.

Thus, we can perceive from this the purpose of creation.

# Tablet of Hermes

...find the truth of the Spirit from the Alpha and Omega <note>original: "A. und Ω."; the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing Christ as the beginning and the end of all things.</note>, and from the spirit and the letter, united together in a single sense. But before the Church can do this, she must be placed into a **crucible** <note>A heat-resistant container used for melting metals; here, a metaphor for a period of intense trial or purification.</note> of great tribulations, so that she may gain a hunger for God and His Word, and learn to research and separate the <term>Subtle</term> from the <term>Coarse</term> letter <note>The author distinguishes between the "dead" literal meaning of scripture and its living, spiritual essence.</note>. Amidst these things, God Himself will separate the godless, as the coarse refuse of the human race, and clear them away from the earth. Only then will the **ascending and descending** of the spirit rightly occur, and we shall be made perfect, and be **tinctured** <note>In alchemy, a "tincture" is a potent substance that can transform and color other materials; spiritually, it refers to being infused with the divine nature.</note> in soul and body with the **Spirit of Life** from God. When, at the end of the universal Church, the coarse physical essence is finally separated from the subtle essence of our bodies, our bodies—because of this tincture—will endure in the final fire of separation and suffer no pain from it; the coarse essence, however, shall be consumed by the fire.

->§. 6.<-

**Thus you will possess all the glory of the whole world, and all ignorance and incapacity shall depart from you. This is the strongest strength of all strength, for it overcomes every subtle thing and penetrates every solid.**

This is by no means intended in its full sense to refer to the **Philosopher's Stone**, but only partially, as if in an image. The **Philosopher's Stone** was indeed, until now, the most precious thing among all worldly things on earth, and its medicine took away the incapacity found in sickness. It also possesses the strongest and most subtle spiritual nature among all external things, and penetrates into metals for their improvement. But for all that, it is still far from being the "strongest strength of all strength," and it does not penetrate every solid thing. The <term>Adepts</term> <note>original: "Adepti"; those who have mastered the secrets of alchemy and successfully created the Stone.</note> also by no means possessed all the glory of the world, but often had to live amidst great persecutions and pursuits. ### The Tablet of Hermes <note>original: *Tabula Hermetis*, the foundational text of Hermeticism also known as the Emerald Tablet.</note>

...all ignorance and incapacity had departed from them, for even the wisest among them were still just as blind to many things as other men, although they possessed great wisdom in some matters. Many of them, however, possessed no wisdom at all, as their writings testify, but were merely proud, puffed-up chatterers who parroted these things from the Tablet of Hermes without understanding what was meant by them. Thus writes Hermogenes <note>A name often associated with Hermetic or alchemical manuscripts, sometimes used as a pseudonym for various authors in the "Hermetic" tradition.</note>: If one were to take a single grain of the Medicine of the Stone <note>The "Medicine of the Stone" refers to the transformative substance produced from the Philosopher's Stone, believed to cure all diseases and enlighten the mind.</note>, one's understanding of all things would be opened, and all ignorance and incapacity would flee away. Likewise, one could represent pillars in the air in which one could see whatever one desired, and more of the like. Yet, for all his medicine, he did not understand, nor could he see, that he would one day be brought to light as a know-nothing chatterer. In the same way, many have made great boasts about how they could see the creation of the world and the creation of Adam within their Stone; however, they understood as much about the creation of the world as a blind man understands of color. Such people have been a true shame and dishonor to the Stone of the Wise <note>The Philosopher's Stone (*Lapis Philosophorum*).</note>; therefore, it is right and just that they, in turn, be brought to shame and mockery by the Stone.

The aforementioned things do not apply to the natural stone <note>The physical or material aspect of the alchemical work.</note> among the <term>adepts</term> <note>Practitioners who have successfully "attained" the secrets of alchemy.</note> of former times, but rather they apply to the spiritual stone in the true doctrine. To the one who would find this, all the glory of this world has been promised; and yet, the natural stone is also not excluded, for it is indeed found by that very same person, and thus both are meant together here: the spiritual stone, Jesus Christ, and the natural stone as one. The Messiah, and all who will be with Him in the world to come, will have all the glory of the whole world, and all ignorance and incapacity will depart from them,

### The Tablet of Hermes <note>original: *Tabula Hermetis*, referring to the Emerald Tablet, a cryptic foundational text of alchemy and Hermeticism.</note>

...[reach]ing, for all of nature on earth will itself be a **Stone of Blessing**, which suffers neither disease nor ruin, because it will be permeated by the holy essence of God. This Stone is the strongest strength of all strength, for it constantly increases in power and might, and will penetrate all subtlety and also all that is best, so that even the subtle satanic nature together with all coarse physicality must give way, for it can dissolve the gates of death and the strong bonds of hell. Whoever reaches and experiences only the first beginning of this glory also attains the greatest glory of the world, for he proceeds ever from one glory into another, and whoever overcomes now shall inherit everything.

In the church and in doctrine, the teaching of the **O. C.** <note>Likely an abbreviation for *Ordo Crucis* (Order of the Cross) or a related Rosicrucian group.</note> is this Stone. The outer religions and teachings were also stones, and were very strong, but this is the strongest strength of all strength, and it overcomes all subtlety and all that is best of the falsely celebrated art of the wisdom of this world. All lack of understanding and incapacity must give way to it. And whoever finds this Stone has all the glory of the whole world to look forward to, for he is the **Solomon of the final age** <note>A prophetic figure in esoteric tradition expected to appear at the end of time to restore true wisdom and the Temple.</note>.

->§. 7.<-

Thus was the world created, and therefore strange unions occur, and various wonders are worked, to act in this manner. And for this reason I am called **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>The "Thrice-Greatest Hermes," a legendary sage said to have lived in ancient Egypt and authored the Hermetic corpus.</note>, because I possess the three parts of the wisdom of the whole world. It is fulfilled, what I have said concerning the **work of the sun** <note>A reference to the final operation in alchemy (the *Opus Solis*), which produces gold or spiritual perfection.</note>.

The creation of the world—both the initial creation and the creative work until now, as well as the coming new creation—is the primary meaning of this **Tablet of Hermes**, for here we are directed toward the creation of the world. The Stone of the

### Tablet of Hermes <note>original: *Tabula Hermetis*</note>

...[to the] wise is also meant by this at the same time, because it stands in the form of the creation of the world. The world has had three external main creations, and the Stone has three rotations <note>original: *Rotationen*. In alchemy, these are the repeated cycles of distillation or "cooking" that the matter must undergo to reach perfection.</note> in the first work of its founding. The Stone thus bears witness that the world really did have such three creations, and that I have correctly described the creation of the world. Creation, in turn, also bears witness that this is the true **Philosopher’s Stone**, because in its preparation it stands in the form of the creation of the world. It has the three parts of air in the empty space of the vessel, and has the three parts in the matter animated by air, as well as the three rotations, and thus it also stands in the form of the church in the three dispensations <note>original: *Haushaltungen*. A theological term referring to the different "administrations" or eras of God’s dealing with humanity, such as the eras of the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.</note>. These three—God with the world, the church, and the Stone—belong together as one in this **Tablet of Hermes**; therefore it is said: I am called **Hermes Thrice-Greatest** <note>original: *Hermes Trismegiſtus*</note> because I possess the three parts of the wisdom of the whole world. **Hermes** means an interpreter or orator, and **Trismegistus** means a threefold or tripled one. Therefore, the **Tablet of Hermes** must have a threefold interpretation, because it speaks of three things, and to this also belongs a threefold wisdom, namely: the wisdom of the Word of God in its true sense, from which flows the wisdom of the knowledge of **Jesus Christ** <note>original: *Jeſu Chriſti*</note> and the creation of the world; and thirdly, the wisdom of the **Philosopher’s Stone**. Whoever possesses these three together is **Hermes** at the end of the world.

The words, *thus was the world created*, prove firstly that no human, but God himself wrote this **Tablet**, for before me no human knew the mystery of **Christ** and the creation of the world—which both belong together as one—how then could he have said: "thus was the world created"? If someone had known it, then God’s Word would not be true, which says that the mystery of **Christ** had been hidden from the beginning of the world and should only [be revealed] at the end together with

<meta>This page continues the critique of previous alchemists (Adepts) and their superficial understanding of the Tablet of Hermes. ### Tablet of Hermes <note>original: *Tabula Hermetis*</note>

<column-break/>

...be revealed in all mysteries. It is also obvious and well-known enough that no one has ever truly recognized the true Jesus Christ and the true meaning of the Word of God; therefore, no one has recognized the mystery of creation either, and for that reason, no man, but God Himself, wrote the **Tablet of Hermes**.

Secondly, these words prove that all the **Adepts** <note>original: *Adepti*. These are the "masters" of alchemy who claimed to have discovered the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, however many of them have written in their works that one could see the creation of the world from the Stone: I say that they have collectively only parroted these words of Hermes, and that they all understood just as much about the mystery of creation as the simplest peasant, and not a hair more. What do they expect to see from this salt? Nothing other than that the spirit of the world thickens and, as it were, steps out of the invisible into the visible and takes on a body. But even the simplest person can read that very thing from the Scriptures—namely, that visible things emerged from the invisible—and thus the **Adept** had no advantage at all over the simpleton.

The three main creations of the world, and the nature of Adam, no man before me has recognized; indeed, people did not even recognize the three **dispensations** <note>original: *Haushaltungen*. A theological term for the different eras of God's interaction with humanity.</note> of the church, for the heathens were considered to be without God and without the Word of God. What, then, were the **Adepts** able to see of creation in the Stone? Absolutely nothing. For whoever has no wisdom in the spirit sees no more in the Stone than one sees in a common masonry stone.

Thirdly, if the Stone is made in the same way the world was created, then this is also proof that the **Philosopher's Stone** is not made with a coal fire, for the world was not cooked in a coal fire. Thus you see that **Elias Artista** <note>A legendary figure in alchemy predicted to appear in the "last days" to reveal all hidden secrets of the arts and sciences.</note> speaks in a completely different language than all the other **Adepts** who lived before him, and it must be so, for he must surely distinguish himself from others by something.

<meta>This page continues the argument that previous alchemists (Adepts) failed to understand the deeper theological and structural mysteries of creation. ### The Tablet of Hermes <note>original: *Tabula Hermetis*</note>

The actual mystery of creation consists in the **threefold number** <note>original: *Dreyzahl*. This refers to the Trinity or a triple-structure inherent in all things according to theosophical thought.</note>, for one must know that there have been three main creations, and for what purpose or why it had to be so. When one first knows this, only then can one see it in the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of alchemy, here used as a physical map of spiritual reality.</note>, but not before. Therefore, this **Tablet of Hermes** was made and preserved for **Elias Artista** <note>A legendary figure predicted by Paracelsus who would arrive in the "last days" to perfect the arts and reveal all secrets of alchemy and nature.</note>, so that he might show the creation and the entire structure of the world through it, as well as the renewal and restoration of all things: and that he might show the harmony that it has with **Jesus Christ**, and how it comes from Him, being His image and likeness in outer nature, so that from this one might also recognize that God made and sent Elias to reveal the true **Jesus Christ** to all nations.

In a general sense <note>original: *Generaliter*</note>, however, one can see from the **Stone** that just as the spirit of the world takes on a body and makes itself physical; even so, God becomes a man in every human being through the act of creation, as the powers of God in nature form themselves into human creatures; therefore, we are of His essence and His offspring, according to **Acts 17** <note>original: *Actor 17*. Refers to the biblical passage Acts 17:28–29, "For in him we live, and move, and have our being... For we are also his offspring."</note>. But even the **Adepts** <note>original: *Adepti*. Those who claimed to have mastered the secrets of alchemy.</note> have never truly grasped this. Through the various created natures of the world, which had to work into and flow into one another, strange unions occurred, and by working in this manner, many wonders were performed. For the creation of human beings, both good and evil had to work together, even the satanic nature, which had to provide the **contrary force** <note>original: *Contrarium*. A technical term in dialectics and alchemy meaning an opposing force necessary for creation or motion.</note> required for creation. It was wonder enough that human creatures were created in this way. But now at the end, in the return of creation from the curse back into the blessing, we shall first see the true wonders. Of this, the **Stone** is an image for us, since through the wonderful arrangement of its parts and their interaction with one another, such a marvelous Stone is produced, which takes away all diseases and turns the lesser metals into gold. Gold is the king among the metals, and so Christ shall in the future make the lowly, the poor, and the miserable into kings and priests in His kingdom.

### The Tablet of Hermes <note>original: *Tabula Hermetis*</note>

The three parts of the wisdom of the whole world—namely, the aforementioned threefold wisdom—is the **Sun**, for the teaching of the Church is also a **Sun** <note>In this mystical tradition, the Sun represents the source of spiritual illumination and divine truth.</note>. Therefore, the conclusion of this **Tablet** is: *It is fulfilled, what I have said concerning the Work of the Sun* <note>original: "Es ist erfüllet, was ich gesagt habe vom Werk der Sonnen." This is a translation of the final line of the *Tabula Smaragdina* (Emerald Tablet), a foundational hermetic text.</note>. This can be said with truth, for the mystery of the creation of the world has been described: the mystery of the Church in its **three dispensations** <note>original: *Haushaltungen*. Refers to the three historical eras of God's interaction with humanity: the Age of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit.</note> has been described: and the **Philosophers’ Stone** has been described; therefore, the **Work of the Sun** is now fulfilled. The Sun itself is also a great mystery, in that the solar system contains six orbiting planets within itself. It is the throne for the governance of the world, and there dwells our God and Father **Adam**, **Jesus Christ**, and He rules all things <note>The author presents a theosophical view where Christ is the "Second Adam," the cosmic ruler of the restored world.</note>. This has also been described in the "Book of the World," and thus the **Work of the Sun** is also completed or fulfilled in this regard—namely, that which has been said about it is fulfilled. Now, because God has promised all the glory of the whole world to whoever attains this **threefold Philosophers’ Stone**, such glory cannot be far off; I hold God to His promise and await it, because He has given me the three parts of the wisdom of the whole world, and for that reason, I also call myself

->Hermes Trismegistus.<- <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure, "Hermes the Thrice-Greatest," to whom the Emerald Tablet is attributed.</note>

# Chapter Eight.
## Concerning the Stone in Relation to the Planets and Metals.

->§. 1.<-

Because the **Philosophers’ Stone** is a true image of the entire world, which is still continually in the process of creation, and since the planets are the

--- Page 215 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the spiritual and historical interpretation of the Emerald Tablet to the practical and symbolic correspondences of the "Stone" (the Philosophers' Stone). It bridges the gap between macrocosmic forces (planets) and microcosmic materials (chemical substances like Tartar and Saltpeter).</meta>

### Comparison of the Stone with the Planets and Metals

Since these are the main driving forces in creation, the parts of the **Stone** are also assigned to the planets and the metals. Thus, the **Stone** also reveals the seven pillars upon which the wisdom of God has built the house of the world (Proverbs 9:1). The **Moon** is the primary ruler of our lower world, for it circles our Earth, and through it and its orbit, the higher forces are guided to the Earth. In the **Stone**, **Tartar** <term>Tartar: A potassium salt deposit found in wine barrels; in alchemy, it represents the "fixed" or earthly principle.</term> is the Earth, and **Saltpeter** <term>Saltpeter: Potassium nitrate; here it represents the "volatile" or celestial principle that carries the life-force.</term> is the **Moon**. Through the **Saltpeter**, the **World Spirit** <note>original: *Weltgeist* (Spiritus Mundi). A vital, invisible essence believed to permeate the universe and facilitate chemical transformation.</note> must also be brought to the **Tartar**. From this single observation, one can already see that the parts of the **Stone** are not assigned to the planets without a natural reason.

There are essentially only two physical parts, the **Saltpeter** and **Tartar**, which are like man and wife; these then give birth to the child as the third part—namely, the **Air**. The **Tartar** is the **Sun** within the **Stone**, and it is also the Earth. In the structure of the universe, the **Sun** stands in the center and is the center of all life and all light. The Earth, however, also has a center within the structure of the universe—namely, the middle circle among the planetary orbits—and it is the center for creation, because all things are created on Earth.

The **Tartar** is compared to two centers: the center of life, and the center of hell and death. It possesses this fundamentally within itself, for through the **Air**, the center of life is unlocked within it; but before that, it is death. Just as it is compared to the two centers of the world, so too is it twofold within the **Stone**: namely, it exists in the matter animated by **Air**, and it is added once more as the **ferment** <term>ferment: A substance used to "leaven" or trigger a transformation in the alchemical work, similar to yeast in bread.</term>. When the **Air** is in the **Tartar**, there is a **Sun** in the Earth, and the "Above" is in the "Below," or Heaven is in the Earth. Therefore, **Tartar** is the fundamental and primary subject of the entire **Stone**, and this "hell" is a cause and a foundation of the Kingdom of Heaven. The **Sun** has the sign of the **Lion**; therefore, the **Tartar** is also called the **Lion**, who must struggle with the **Eagle** and overcome it <note>The "Lion" often represents the fixed, solid matter, while the "Eagle" represents the volatile, gaseous spirit. Their "struggle" describes the chemical process of stabilization.</note>.

§. 2.
